- 2026-09-01 **The `NextPos`/`PrevPos` asymmetry is kept, and what the *public* surface does when `beginning`/`length` cuts a surrogate pair is pinned instead.** The three options the S26 slice file offered were to make the two symmetric, to keep the asymmetry and document it, or to defer it to Phase 6. Kept, for two reasons. The disagreement is only reachable above `TextEnd`, which the engine never asks about, so a bound test in `PrevPos` would buy nothing observable and sits in the inner loop of every reverse step and every `CharBefore`; and a change with no test that can go red is not a change this repo can protect. What *was* missing is the semantics one layer up, and that turned out to have an oracle after all: Python cannot express an `endpos` inside a character, but it can hold a `str` whose last character is a lone surrogate, which is exactly the string a cut slice denotes. Measured against regex 2026.7.19: `regex.search('.', '\ud83d')` spans (0, 1), `regex.search('a.', 'a\ud83d')` spans (0, 2) and `regex.search('(?r).', 'a\ud83d')` spans (1, 2) - a lone surrogate is one character in both directions. This port answers the same for the equivalent cut, and `MatchSpineTests.A_length_that_cuts_a_surrogate_pair_leaves_a_lone_surrogate_that_matches_as_one_character` pins it in both directions with the pair-intact case beside it. **The general rule: when an internal asymmetry is unreachable, the thing worth spending a test on is the observable semantic above it, not the asymmetry.**
- 2026-09-01 **The oracle consumer ran every row with `InfiniteMatchTimeout`, so a row this port could not finish was a hung run rather than a red one.** Found the hard way while re-running S17's negative control: a mutant sat in `dotnet test` for six minutes on a 1,200-row wave the honest engine answers in 589ms, and the harness printed nothing at all - which looks exactly like a slow build. Now ten seconds per row, which turns a runaway into an ordinary divergence with its pattern and subject in the report. Generous by two orders of magnitude: every generator's subjects are eight characters or fewer, and a row upstream itself could not answer quickly would have stalled the recorder first. **The general rule for a differential harness: the reference side has a timeout by construction, because a human is watching it record; the port side needs one written down, or the harness's failure mode is silence.**
- 2026-09-01 **A negative control is reproducible only if its notes name the mutation exactly and the seed it ran at, and S17's named neither.** Re-running all 37 Phase 3 controls from the closing notes: 34 reproduced their recorded figure exactly at the recorded seed, a 35th (S19-A) reproduced only once that recorded figure disambiguated which of two sites its notes meant, and S17's two could not be reproduced as described at all. "Inverting `InSetSymDiff`'s toggle" has two readings, and the one that flips the *condition* is a **no-op for any even-membered set** - every `~~` atom the generator emits has exactly two members - so it diverged on 0 of 1,200 rows where the notes recorded 6. Inverting the accumulator's initial value fires: 10 at seed 7 and 19 at a fresh seed. Neither number can be checked against 6, because S17 recorded no seed. **Two rules from this.** A control's notes must quote the before/after as the file reads plus the seed - already the standing rule, and S17 predates it. And **prefer a mutation whose effect cannot cancel**: a condition flip inside an XOR fold, a sign flip on a value that is then squared, or an off-by-one on a bound the data never reaches are all mutations that measure the generator's luck rather than the wave's reach.
- 2026-09-01 **An analyzer set strong enough to be worth having will refuse some mutations, so a negative control has to be chosen to keep the code compiling.** S17's second control - "swapping the ASCII property table for the Unicode one" - was written as deleting the ASCII substitution from `Encodings.HasProperty`, which leaves `_asciiMax` unused and fails the build on S1144 as an error. The harness then reports no verdict line, which is indistinguishable from a control that fired until you read the build log. Rewritten as swapping which encoding gets the substitution (`CaseEncoding.Ascii` to `CaseEncoding.Unicode`), it keeps every symbol used and expresses the same fault: 138 divergences of 1,200 at seed 7 and 106 at a fresh seed, against the 133 recorded. **So the driver script must treat a missing report as a failure and print the build tail**, which is now what `.scratch/run-controls.py` does, and a control that deletes a code path should be re-expressed as one that redirects it.
- 2026-09-01 **`subprocess.run(timeout=...)` does not bound `dotnet test`, because the timeout kills the direct child and then blocks for ever draining a pipe its grandchildren still hold.** A 240-second bound sat unfired for six minutes: `dotnet test` spawns MSBuild nodes and a test host that inherit the captured `stdout` handle, and `run` calls `communicate()` again after the kill. Redirecting the child's output to a *file* instead of a pipe fixes it. Worth carrying because every future harness script in this repo drives `dotnet` from Python or PowerShell: **capture through a pipe only where you are prepared for the timeout not to work.**
- 2026-09-01 **The pinned oracle question was settled by source identity rather than by a CI run, which is the stronger answer.** S26 owed a re-check of S15's five rejections against the CI-built 2026.8.12 rather than the PyPI 2026.7.19. Neither route was available in the session - `gh` is deliberately outside the driver's allowlist so an unattended slice cannot post, and a local build fails for want of MSVC ("Microsoft Visual C++ 14.0 or greater is required") - but the delta between the two releases is one commit, `1760a20`, and `git diff 1760a20~1 1760a20 -- src/ regex/_regex_core.py` is **empty**: the whole change is a version string, a classifier and a CI matrix line. The C engine that raises `RuntimeError: invalid RE code` is byte-identical in both builds, so a run against 2026.7.19 *is* a run against 2026.8.12 for this question, and all five still reject. **The general rule: before booking a CI run to compare two versions, diff the two versions - a proof that the code cannot differ beats an observation that it did not.**
- 2026-09-01 **The prefilter contingency is closed with no prefilter ported: nothing in the suite is slow, and the two complexity guards are the reason.** S19 opened it in case a ported test had to be skipped for timeout without `locate_required_string`, and S26 widened it to cover a test that merely passes *slowly*. Measured from the final ratchet's own TRX: 5,716 results in 38.7s of test time, of which **10 take a second or more and every one of those is either a Unicode table sweep over the whole codepoint space or a deliberate complexity guard**. Both guards pass - 0.463s for the BMP subject and 0.588s for the astral one, over 240,000 code units each. So the prefilters stay Phase 7 work, where they belong as optimisation behind benchmarks, and `try_match`'s predicate dispatch with them. **And the guard was watched failing, which corrected the recorded control**: forcing `MatchState.OneUnitPerCharacter` false does *not* make it fail (the class runs in 1.2s; the two tests that fail are the astral-correctness ones), because that only moves a BMP subject off the restored arithmetic and onto the sampled position table, which is also linear. Re-expressed as the defect that actually existed - `StepBy` and `CountBetween` converting by walking - the guard fails in **20s 285ms** with `RegexMatchTimeoutException` against 0.493s unmutated, so the 20-second ceiling really is enforced by the engine's own timeout (a `ushort` counter bumped by 0x100 per dispatch iteration polls the deadline every 256 iterations). **The general rule: mutating a fast-path *selector* tests the fast path, not the algorithm - to find out whether a complexity guard is load bearing, mutate the thing whose complexity it asserts.**
- 2026-09-11 **A capability tag on the board is a hypothesis until the tests behind it have been run.** At the Phase 4 checkpoint every remaining `needs:` tag was probed by removing its `[Skip]` attributes and running the suite: 92 skipped tests already passed - the whole of `inline-flags`, `version-flags`, `comments`, `branch-reset` and `possessive`, `(*FAIL)`, and 17 of the 20 `named-lists` tests - because their skip prose described a parser that did not exist yet when Phase 1 wrote it. Upstream never had a `STRING_SET` opcode (`StringSet` lowers to `BRANCH`, `_regex_core.py:4069`) nor a possessive one (`PossessiveRepeat._compile` emits `ATOMIC` + the greedy repeat, `:3034`), so both families were finished by S13, S19 and S20 without anyone noticing. The four "unclaimed" families S26 flagged collapse to `(*PRUNE)`/`(*SKIP)`, 30 tests, which Phase 4 takes. Un-skipped as between-slice maintenance; three named-list tests retagged to the lookaround or fuzzy tag their pattern actually needs. Rule for every future phase close: probe every tag on the board this way before writing the handover - it is a ten-second run per tag.
- 2026-09-11 **Before believing a negative control, check that it fires; a control at zero measures nothing, and the way it gets to zero is by mutating a flag nobody reads.** S27's control C was meant to express "the lookbehind body is compiled forwards" and was first written as `subargs.Forward = forward` to `subargs.Forward = args.Forward` in `NodeCompiler.BuildLookaround`. It found **0 divergences in 1200 rows** across two seeds, which looked at first like a thin generator. It was not: `grep -n Forward src/FuzzyRegex/Engine/NodeCompiler.cs` shows `args.Forward` is read at four lines only (`:1424`, `:1432`, `:1488`, `:1495`), all inside the repeat builders, so the field was never the lookbehind direction mechanism at all. The direction lives in the *parser* - `LookAround.Compile` passes `Behind` to `Subpattern.Compile` (`Parsing/Nodes.cs:3488`), so a lookbehind body arrives at the compiler already reversed. Repointed there, the same control finds 25 and 29 of 600. **The general rule: a control that does not fire is a finding about the control first and the generator second - trace the field you mutated to its readers before widening anything.** This is the mirror of the 2026-09-01 entry above, which warns against mutations whose effect can cancel; this one warns against mutations that never reach the behaviour they name.
- 2026-09-11 **A lookaround generator has to emit multi-atom bodies and a branch that is given up mid-attempt, or two of its three controls sit at one divergence in six hundred.** S27's generator began with single-atom lookaround bodies only, and controls A (the backtrack arm dropping `text_pos`) and B (the lookaround never saving its captures) found 1-2 rows of 600. The diagnosis is mechanical rather than statistical. A single-atom body either matches or fails **without having moved**, so it never leaves `text_pos` displaced and the restore that the backtrack arm exists for has nothing to restore; and a lookaround reached once per match attempt is never *unwound*, so whatever it undoes is never observed being undone. Adding a `sequence` body kind (two or three atoms, the first drawn from the subject) took A to 12 and 7; adding an `alt-with-look` piece - `(?:LOOK atom|atom)`, a branch leading with a lookaround beside a fallback - took B to 7 and 6. **The general rule for any construct that saves and restores state: the generator must reach it on a path that is abandoned, not only on one that succeeds, and the construct's body must be able to consume before it fails.**
- 2026-09-11 **`pop_repeats` is nearly unobservable to a differential wave, because a repeat's own backtrack entries restore the same state along the same path.** S28 ported `push_repeats`/`pop_repeats` with `CONDITIONAL` and built a `conditionals` generator for it. The branch-selection controls fire hard - 29/42, 17/32 and 46/53 divergences of 600 at two seeds - but control C, which corrupts the saved repeat count, sits at **2 and 8**, and control E, which stops the guard lists being restored, at **0 and 1**. Two widenings failed to move C: raising `repeat-of-cond` from weight 12 to 24 with a forced repeating quantifier, and then changing the mutation so a zero count would also bite. The reason is structural. `GREEDY_REPEAT` saves its own count on the bstack and the conditional is unwound along that same bstack, so the conditional's copy is mostly restoring what the repeat machinery would have restored anyway; only an *outer* repeat, mid-iteration, whose count is non-zero when the `CONDITIONAL` is entered can show the difference. **The rule for S30, which reuses the identical mechanism for `GROUP_CALL`/`GROUP_RETURN`: a green conditionals-shaped wave is evidence that the branch selection is right and says almost nothing about the repeat stack - cover that half with a generator built for it, or not at all, but do not claim it.**
- 2026-09-11 **`tools/run-controls.py` caches its waves, so a widened generator silently re-measures the old one.** The cache is `.scratch/control-waves/<generator>-<count>-<seed>.jsonl` and it is keyed on none of the generator's content. S28 widened `conditionals`, re-ran control C and got figures byte-identical to the run before the widening - which reads exactly like "the widening had no effect" and is in fact "the widening was not measured". Deleting the two cached waves gave different numbers immediately. **Delete the cached wave for any generator you have just changed, before you believe a control's figure.** This is the same class of error as the skill's existing rule that a control run partway through a slice measures a generator that no longer exists; this one is the mechanism by which it happens without anyone editing anything.
- 2026-09-11 **`findall` and `finditer` are not the same loop upstream, and S25's measurement could not have told them apart.** S25 recorded them as "measurably the same function - verified 2026-09-01 ... on nine subject/pattern pairs including the overlapped, zero-width and reverse cases", and built `Iteration.Scan` on `pattern_findall`. Only `pattern_findall` carries a `slice_start <= text_pos <= slice_end` loop guard (`:22415`); `scanner_search_or_match` (`:20903`), `pattern_subx` (`:21859`) and `pattern_split` (`:22285`) rely on `do_match`'s own test (`:18128`), which compares `text_pos` against `slice_end` going forward and **never** against `slice_start`. The guard can only bite when something moves `slice_start` mid-scan, and the only thing that does is `(*SKIP)` - which S25 had no way to compile, so its nine pairs were all in the region where the two genuinely agree. Measured against regex 2026.7.19: `regex.findall(r'[A-Z]*(*SKIP)_', '__BB__B', overlapped=True)` gives 3 matches and `regex.finditer` on the same arguments gives 6, and the six are the correct ones - each equals `p.match('__BB__B', k)` for k = 0..5. `Iteration.Scan` and `Iteration.Next` lose the guard and `MatchState.IsInSlice` is deleted. **The general rule: "I measured these two code paths and they agree" is only as strong as the inputs that could have separated them - before recording two upstream functions as identical, find the input that reaches the line where their source differs, and if you cannot compile that input yet, record the difference rather than the agreement.**
- 2026-09-11 **S29 is parked, not done: `(*SKIP)` does not commit the search position, and the mechanism is not understood.** `regex.compile(r'(?:..(*SKIP)x|q)x').search('ab cd xx')` is `None` upstream and stable across repeats, while `.match('ab cd xx', 4)` is `(4, 8)` - upstream finds that match only when the search starts at 4, because a failed attempt in which a `(*SKIP)` fired commits the search past position 4. This port re-tries it and returns `(4, 8)` from `search`. Forward direction, plain `search`, no `(?r)` and no multi-match operation, so it is the core semantics of the verb. It was not fixed because a hand trace of upstream's `FAILURE` advance (`:15695`-`:15738`, whose `text_pos < slice_start` clamp *is* ported, at `Matcher.cs:5334`) predicts upstream *should* match at 4 and it does not - and guessing in `basic_match`'s search-advance path risks 5394 passing tests for a cell no ported test covers. The 32 ported verb tests all pass, so the defect is invisible to upstream's own suite and was found only by the new `verbs` generator. **`verbs` is therefore kept out of `run-oracle.ps1`'s default list**, because a generator that is red for a known parked reason would turn every later slice's oracle run red and hide that slice's own result; the parameter's doc comment says to put it back the moment this is fixed. **The general rule: when a slice's central construct passes every ported test and fails the oracle, the ported suite was never evidence for that construct - park it with the reproduction rather than shipping the tag.**
- 2026-09-11 **`tools/run-oracle.ps1 -SkipRecord` silently ignores `-Rows`.** `-Rows` is consumed only inside the `if (-not $SkipRecord)` block, so `-Rows candidate.jsonl -SkipRecord` re-compares whatever wave is already at `TestResults/oracle/wave.jsonl` and reports a confident GREEN for it. It did exactly that here, against S28's leftover `conditionals` wave, and the only tell was the `waves:` line naming a generator and seed nobody had asked for. **Read the `waves:` line, not just the verdict** - it is the one part of the report that says which rows were actually compared.
- 2026-09-11 **S29's `(*SKIP)` divergence is upstream's required-string prefilter, not the verb, and the port's answer is the correct one.** `regex.compile(r"(?:..(*SKIP)x|q)x").search("ab cd xx")` is `None` but `.search(s, 4)` is `(4, 8)`; removing the required literal (`[xy]` for the final `x`) or planting an early `x` in the subject gives `(4, 8)` from 0; `..(*SKIP)xx` on `cd xxx` matches at 1, below the skip point, because a fixed `req_offset` lets `locate_required_string` (`_regex.c:11082`) jump past the attempt in which the verb would have fired. Same mechanism as upstream #612. The port deliberately has no prefilter until Phase 7, so this is an intentional divergence to record, not a fix to make - and it is the first concrete case for the strict allowlist ROADMAP specifies for Phase 6. Owner to decide whether that mechanism is pulled forward into S29.
- 2026-09-11 **Correction to the entry above, after a blind review and the decisive probe: the mechanism is the fixed-offset required-string jump, and it is not a bug on either side.** Upstream compiles `(?:..(*SKIP)x|q)x` with `req_offset=3, req_chars="x"` and `locate_required_string` therefore starts the first attempt at `found_pos - 3`, from which `(*SKIP)` steps past position 4. Perl does the identical thing (`rx_origin now 3` in its debug trace) and agrees with upstream on every probe; PCRE2 documents the class. Neutralising upstream's prefilter from Python (`req_offset=-1, req_chars=None`) turns every divergent answer into the port's, and 450 verb rows with no literal at all agree outright. The port is therefore right for an engine without a prefilter, and Phase 7's faithful port of `locate_required_string` will bring it into line with upstream. Until then the `verbs` generator is recorded against the prefilter-free upstream, tagged, with Phase 7 obliged to remove the wrapper. No upstream report. The blind review reported the claim falsified; its counter-cases were each explained by the optimiser trace, which is the reminder that a reviewer's verdict is a hypothesis too.
- 2026-09-11 **Second correction, and the one that closes S29: the required-string prefilter explains the *parked reproduction* and explains **none** of the four oracle-wave rows. There are two mechanisms, and they were filed as one.** The entry above predicted that recording `verbs` against a prefilter-free upstream would make the wave green. Tested before it was believed: the prefilter-free wrapper changes **0 rows of 3600** (seeds 7, 20260913, 4242, 1, 20260911, each row recorded both ways and compared), and the four divergences survive it unchanged. Their real cause is **upstream's `search_start`** (`_regex.c:8385`), the fast scan for the next plausible start position, which `basic_match` takes whenever the start test has a `search_start_*` twin (`:11819`). The two halves disagree about the slice: `search_start_END_OF_LINE_rev` (`:8055`) bounds itself with `text_end`, while `try_match_END_OF_LINE` (`:7108`) - the predicate `basic_match` itself consults - bounds itself with `slice_end`. Nothing but a `(*SKIP)` moves the slice inside an attempt, so the two agree on every pattern there is; once one does, upstream's fast path walks past a start position its own slow path would accept, and this port, which has only the slow path, tries it. Minimised to `regex.finditer(r"(?r)(?:a*(*SKIP)b|[^a-f])$", "\nb", regex.M)` - upstream one match, this port two - and confirmed by emulating the scanner in front of each attempt, which reproduces upstream exactly on every case including one where it must *not* lose upstream's second match. Isolating ladder: needs `(*SKIP)` (a `(*PRUNE)` agrees), needs `(?r)`, needs a multi-match operation, and needs `$` with MULTILINE - `\Z` and `$` without it agree, because `END_OF_STRING_LINE`'s two halves both use `text_end`. Same class as the prefilter and the same treatment: not a bug on either side, deferred to Phase 7, pinned by gap tests marked `PHASE 7: INVERT THIS TEST`, and `verbs` stays off the default generator list until `search_start` lands. **PORTMAP's two rows calling these prefilters "semantically transparent ... answers the same" are corrected: that is true only of patterns that cannot move the slice mid-attempt.** **The general rule: a root cause that explains the case you minimised is not thereby the cause of the rows you did not minimise - re-run the wave against the fix before you write down that it fixes the wave.**
- 2026-09-11 **A negative control's raw divergence count is meaningless when the honest engine already diverges on that wave; record the baseline beside it.** S29's controls were recorded as totals (A: 0 / 1 / 1). Measuring the same cached waves through the *unmutated* consumer gives a baseline of 0 / 3 / 0 / 1 at seeds 7 / 20260913 / 4242 / 314159, so control A's true delta is 0 / **-2** / +1 / 0 - at one seed its mutation *removes* two divergences, because widening the slice back to the whole text is exactly what neutralises the `search_start` asymmetry it happens to sit next to. A control that runs negative is not a thin control, it is a broken one, and no total could have shown it. S29-B (+48/+37/+36/+43) and S29-C (+225/+238/+220/+234) are unaffected; S29-D is +2/+1/0/0 and blind at two of four seeds. **Measure the baseline whenever the engine is not known-green on the generator you are controlling with, and record controls as before-and-after pairs.**
- 2026-09-11 **`state->group_call_guard_list` is write-only upstream, so S30 ports none of it and the "Phase 4 allocates it" hole in `MatchState.Create` is closed by deletion rather than by code.** `grep -n group_call_guard_list upstream/src/_regex.c` (2026-09-11) returns exactly six sites: the declaration (`:513`), the allocation and `memset` in `state_init_2` (`:18317-18323`), the reset in `reset_guards` (`:3398-3400`), the per-attempt clear in `basic_match` (`:11797-11803`), and two `re_dealloc` calls in `state_fini` (`:18718-18722`). Not one is a read - no `is_guarded` or `guard` call takes a list from this array, and the only local that aliases one is the clear at `:11800`, which zeroes it. It guards nothing in upstream as shipped. It is now a row in PORTMAP's "deliberately not ported" table with the grep and the date, and a `sync-upstream` check: if `group_call_guard_list` ever appears on the right of a comparison it becomes real state and `GROUP_CALL` has to consult it. **The consequence for slice planning: this also deleted one of the two negative controls S30's slice file specified ("the guard list never cleared"), because a mutation to state nothing reads cannot diverge. Before writing a control, check that the thing it mutates is read** - the same rule the 2026-09-11 S27 entry reached from the other direction.
- 2026-09-11 **`current` and the capture list are separate upstream, and a group call is the first construct in this port that can make them disagree.** `Match.GroupAt` returned an empty capture list for any group with `Current < 0`, which was invisible for three phases because every earlier construct moved the two together - `unsave_capture` decrements `count` as it clears `current`. `GROUP_RETURN` restores the caller's `current` (`pop_groups`, `:2662`) and deliberately leaves the callee's captures on the list, so after `regex.match(r'(?&routine)(?(DEFINE)(?<routine>.))', 'a')` upstream reports `group('routine') is None` **and** `captures('routine') == ['a']`: `match_get_group_by_index` (`:18847`) consults `current`, `match_get_captures_by_index` (`:19137`) walks `count` and never looks at it. Five of S30's sixty tests failed on this, and the fix is one line in `Match.cs`, not in the engine. **The general rule: when the last few tests of a slice all fail the same way and the engine trace is correct, look at the surface that reads the state, not at the code that wrote it.**
- 2026-09-11 **A `(?&name)` call inside a lookbehind matches here and fails upstream, and upstream contradicts itself twice about it - recorded as upstream's defect, next to issue 614, for Phase 6's sweep.** Minimal: `regex.compile(r'(?(DEFINE)(?<a>a))(?<=(?&a))c').match('ac', pos=1)` is `None`; this port gives `(1, 2)`, which is the answer "`a` precedes `c`" asks for. Upstream matches such a lookbehind only when it is the entire pattern. The two inconsistencies that place the defect upstream: its own `search` finds `(2, 3)` on `r'(?(DEFINE)(?<ab>ab))(?<=(?&ab))c'` / `'abcd'` with `captures('ab') == ['ab']` while `match(pos=2)` on that same compiled pattern returns `None`; and with the tail made optional, `c?` and `c*` both return `(1, 2)` - consuming the very `c` that `c`, `c+`, `[c]` and `(?:c)` refuse. Two explanations ruled out by measurement: **not our parser**, since this port's bytecode is upstream's code for code modulo one SET_UNION member's character order that `record-compile-corpus.py` sorts, and upstream's own dump carries the reversed copy of the called group (`CALL_REF 0, GROUP 0 1 1, CHARACTER_REV b, CHARACTER_REV a, END, END`); and **not the required-string prefilter**, since switching upstream's off the way `record-oracle.py` does for `verbs` leaves the answer `None`. Pinned in `Gaps/Engine/GroupCallTests.cs`; the `recursion` generator does not draw the shape, so one known upstream defect cannot colour the whole wave. **Nothing drafted or filed upstream - that needs the owner's approval first.**
- 2026-09-11 **A recursion generator must check every shape in *both* directions, because `(?r)` turns a right-recursive pattern into a left-recursive one.** `\w(?R)?` is safe as written and raises `MemoryError` from upstream the moment `(?r)` is prefixed: reversed, the call leads and the recursion never advances. It was caught before it reached the wave by running every candidate shape through upstream under both directions over a dozen subjects. Anything added to `RECURSION_SHAPES` gets the same check. Related, and the reason it matters more here than elsewhere: neither engine guards left recursion at all - upstream raises `MemoryError`, this port throws from `ByteStack.Grow`'s 1GB `RE_MEMORY_LIMIT` - so the failure is bounded and faithful, but it is a failure, and a generator that emits it produces a wave of errors rather than a wave of answers.
- 2026-09-11 **Widening a generator is not always the way to make a thin control fire; sometimes the answer is rows.** S30's control B (deleting `GROUP_CALL`'s repeat-guard clearing) found 0 of 600 at one seed and 4 at another. Four shapes with a repeat *inside* a called group took it to 5 and 2 - firing at both seeds. Four more shapes made it **worse**, 1 and 0, by diluting the draw, and were reverted. The path is genuinely rare - about one row in three hundred - so the control's count was raised to 2400, where it fires at all three seeds (10 / 5 / 8). **The general rule: when a widening moves a control the wrong way, the construct is rare rather than under-represented, and more rows beat more shapes. Measure after each widening and revert the ones that do not pay** - and delete `.scratch/control-waves/` first, or you will measure the generator you had before.
- 2026-09-12 **The ratchet's "passing" and "baseline" counts differ by design, and the tool now says so.** 107 ported tests have two identical `[Arguments]` rows because upstream's tables do (for example `test_repeat_minmax` repeats `^(\w){3,4}?$`), so they run twice under one id and the baseline, a set of ids, holds 108 fewer entries than the run has passing results. Verified from the TRX: 5462 passed results, 5354 distinct ids, 107 ids with two results. Not a hidden regression - `Test-Ratchet` reds on any failed result regardless of id - but it cost two slices' closing notes and an owner flag, so `check-ratchet.ps1` prints the distinct-id count beside the result count. The duplicate rows stay: they are upstream's, and deleting them would be editing the port's test data to please a tool.
- 2026-09-12 **`Matcher.TryMatch`'s reduction to upstream's default arm is transparent for a FAILURE and NOT for a PARTIAL, so S31 restored the partial half only.** Every caller stops on a negative status, so upstream stops inside the branch or repeat where this port carried on, entered it, and let the opcode raise the partial one step later - by which time an enclosing group had re-closed. `regex.search(r'(\D*?)z', 'a', partial=True)` leaves group 1 unset upstream and had it as (0,1) here. The one-character test nodes are now consulted through `MatchOne` and only a `PARTIAL` is propagated; `FAILURE` still reads as `SUCCESS`, so the Phase 7 fast path S19 measured and reverted (DECISIONS 2026-08-31) is not reinstated. Confined to partial matching by construction - `TryMatchOne` answers `PARTIAL` only when `partial_side` is set - and verified so: 51,000 non-partial rows and 1,980 non-partial `finditer` rows replayed against HEAD and the change with 0 differences.

The static convenience overloads still carry no `partial`, for the same reason they carry no `pos`/`endpos`.
- 2026-09-12 **Three partial-matching divergence families are pinned and parked rather than fixed, and the third one carries the measurement that says why.** (1) Upstream's unported `search_start` prefilter answers `RE_ERROR_PARTIAL` of its own (`:8402`, `:8411`, `:8662`-`:8960`): `regex.compile(r'(?r)\b$').search('', partial=True)` is `(0,0)` partial upstream and `None` here, while upstream's own `match` and `fullmatch` answer `None` - search-only is what identifies the cause. Same mechanism as S29's `verbs` rows; ~1 row in 2,000, so `partial` stays in the default generator list on the measured rate. (2) Half of upstream's partial arms are bounded by `slice_*` and half by `text_*` - `try_match_STRING` (`:7396`) against the `STRING` opcode's own arm - so they only part company once the slice is narrower than the subject; the new `partial-sliced` generator reaches that axis and finds 8 rows in 2,000 at seed 31, every one `(?r)`, and is deliberately off the default list. (3) A bounded lazy repeat loses its partial (`regex.match('ba??x', 'baa', partial=True)` is `(0,3)` upstream, `None` here). **S31 tried the obvious repair and reverted it:** asking the partial guard once more before the loop's limit break, where upstream's specialised arms ask before their own limit check (`:16545-16549`), fixes that family and breaks a larger one, because those arms also cap the limit per tail op (`min(limit, slice_end - 1)`, `:16543`). All three need Phase 7 work the S31 slice file holds out of scope; all three are pinned in `Gaps/Engine/PartialMatchingTests.cs` and marked to invert.
- 2026-09-12 **The oracle row shape gained `pos`/`endpos`, recorded in UTF-16 with the untranslated pair beside them, and follows upstream's negative-index convention rather than clamping.** Upstream's `pos`/`endpos` are codepoint indices and this API's `beginning`/`length` are UTF-16 code units, so handing the consumer the raw pair would narrow a different slice on an astral subject - a divergence about encoding invented by the recorder. `regex` also wraps a negative index like a slice (`search('abc', 0, -1)` searches `[0, 2)`) rather than clamping it, and an `endpos` below `pos` must become an empty slice and not this API's `-1` "no limit" sentinel. All four cases were raised by the S31 second blind pass and are verified by an 8-row edge file covering reversed, negative, one-ended, out-of-range and astral slices.
- 2026-09-12 **`same_values` and `equivalent_nodes` are the required-string prefilter's, not POSIX's, and PORTMAP said otherwise for three phases.** They sit immediately before `save_best_match` in `_regex.c` and were bucketed with it by the 2026-09-01 symbol audit and then by S32's own slice file. Their one caller in the whole file is `:11771`, the `do_search_start` suppression in `basic_match`: a pattern whose required string *is* the start pair's test node needs no start-position scan. So they are Phase 7 with the rest of the prefilter, and S32 ported neither. The lesson generalises: **adjacency in `_regex.c` is not evidence of a family - grep the callers before believing a bucket**, because the same audit also promised the `RE_BestList` four to Phase 4 when they are `do_best_fuzzy_match`'s and stay Phase 5.
- 2026-09-12 **A generator that can emit a catastrophic-backtracking pattern is measuring the build configuration, not the port, and the fix belongs in the generator.** S32's first `posix` generator hung a quantifier on any piece, including pieces that already held one; seed 31 row 1407 was three quantifiers deep over a five-character subject and red the wave on the oracle's 10s row timeout. It was never a divergence - both engines answer four matches, the port in 1.1s and upstream in 0.69s optimised, and the port in 17.9s in the Debug build the consumer runs. POSIX is what exposed it, because it explores every remaining path instead of stopping at the first match. The rule now in `_posix_piece`: **a quantifier goes on a piece only if the piece holds none already.** Raising the row timeout would have been the wrong fix - it hides an exponential pattern rather than not generating one.
- 2026-09-12 **Reverse + a general repeat + a narrowed slice makes `fullmatch` diverge, and S32 pinned it without diagnosing it.** `regex.compile(r'(?r)(ab)+').fullmatch('xabz', 1, 3)` is `None` upstream and matches `(1, 3)` here. All three conditions are needed: `match` and `search` agree, a forward `(ab)+` agrees, `(?r)a+` (a `*_ONE` repeat) agrees, and the whole-subject slice agrees. Same slice-versus-text bounds family as S31's partial rows. Raised by the S32 blind review as an out-of-scope aside, reproduced independently before being acted on, and pinned in `Gaps/Engine/ReverseMatchingTests.cs` with all five measurements quoted. **Deliberately not fixed and deliberately not called upstream's bug**: upstream refusing to fullmatch `'ab'` against the slice that is exactly `'ab'` looks wrong, and that claim needs its own research and second opinion rather than a sentence in a slice that was about something else.
- 2026-09-12 **Owner rule: no known bug ships in this port.** Every bug identified with overwhelming evidence gets fixed before 1.0, whether ours or inherited from upstream; faithful reproduction of an upstream defect is not an exemption. Consequences: a divergence needs a verdict on which side is right (documented definition, a second engine's actual run, a blind review), never just "pinned and parked"; port wrong = fix now, test-first; upstream wrong = pin the port's answer as a permanent test, never inverted when Phase 7 ports the optimisation; Phase 7 ports upstream's prefilters without importing their answers, and a pinned port-is-right test changing is a regression; inherited bugs the port reproduces are Phase 6's fix list and reach 1.0 fixed. Design spec amendment 16.
- 2026-09-12 **The S29 and S31 parked divergences, judged against PCRE2 run directly** (`docs/plan/2026-09-12-divergence-research.md`, `tools/probes/pcre2-partial-and-skip.py`): the port is right on every `(*SKIP)` case (PCRE2 10.47 agrees with and without `NO_START_OPTIMIZE`; Perl's and upstream's answers are their optimisers', and upstream's `(1,5)` on `..(*SKIP)xx`/`cd xxx` retries below the committed position, a bug); right on `ba??x`/`baa` partial (PCRE2 None soft and hard - upstream's partial is a bug); right on `(?r)\b$`/empty search (upstream's own forward path and `match` agree); **wrong on the reversed empty-slice partial**, which S33 fixes. The S31 slice file's "the four rows must now agree" and every inverted in Phase 7 comment were the wrong call and are withdrawn. Two document-derived predictions from a research subagent (PCRE2 would say partial for `ba??x`/`baa` and for an empty subject) were refuted by running PCRE2: **run the binary when one is reachable; a derivation from its manual is a hypothesis.**
- 2026-09-12 **The reversed empty-slice partial was `try_match`'s six `try_match_STRING*` arms, and the reason only the reversed half diverged is that `text_end` and `slice_end` are the same number.** `do_match` sets both to `endpos` (`:18435`), so a forward arm bounded by `slice_end` (`try_match_STRING`, `:7396`) and the `STRING` opcode's own arm bounded by `text_end` (`:14737`) cannot disagree at the top level; `slice_start` is `pos` and `text_start` is always 0, so the reversed pair part company the moment `pos > 0`. Ported as `Matcher.IsStringTestPartial`, reduced to the partial answer only, on S31's terms. **It is an opcode-by-opcode answer and not a rule about `slice_start`:** `try_match_CHARACTER_REV` (`:7137`) keeps the `text_start` bound, so `(?r)a` on `'abc'[1:1]` is `None` in both engines while `(?r)a(bc)*` is a partial in both, and a second gap test pins six such rows so the fix cannot be "simplified" into one bound.
- 2026-09-12 **The reversed narrowed-slice `fullmatch` is upstream's bug, and the mechanism is that upstream has three `match_all` checks that do not agree.** `try_match`'s `RE_OP_SUCCESS` arm (`:7832`) bounds it by `text_start`; `basic_match`'s own `SUCCESS` opcode (`:15167`) and the search loop after a match (`:11880`) both bound it by `slice_start`. Only a GENERAL repeat consults `try_match` for its tail (`:13237`, `:13617`), which is why the symptom needs `(?r)` + `fullmatch` + `pos > 0` + a repeat whose body is not a single character, and why `(?r)a+` (a `*_ONE` repeat) is fine. **Two symptoms, one defect:** the match is lost outright (`(?r)(ab)+` fullmatching `'xabz'[1:3]`), or a complete zero-width match is reported as a partial (`(?r)([\p{L}\p{N}])??` under `F|I` fullmatching `'AAB'[2:2]`, where upstream's own `match` and `search` say complete). The second was the `partial-sliced` wave's row 756 at seed 31 and looked like a separate `\K` family. Port right, tests permanent, reported in `docs/plan/upstream-reports/LEDGER.md`.
- 2026-09-12 **`search_start` cannot be switched off from Python and `locate_required_string` can, which decides how a judged divergence is recorded.** `do_search_start` is set unconditionally at `:25732` and is not one of `_regex.compile`'s arguments; the only lever from outside is `req_string` happening to equal the start test node (`:11770`), which reaches a literal-prefixed pattern and nothing else. `req_offset`/`req_chars` ARE arguments, so `PREFILTER_FREE_GENERATORS` works for the required-string prefilter only. S33 added `partial-sliced` to it after finding that on a partial row that prefilter **suppresses** a partial rather than merely moving an attempt: `regex.compile(r'(?rimf)(x)[\p{L}\p{N}]{2,}?').match('a\na..A', 6, 6, partial=True)` is `None` plain and `((6,6), True)` prefilter-free, and the same pattern without the leading `(x)` - nothing for the prefilter to find - is `((6,6), True)` either way. So plain upstream answers according to whether the pattern happens to carry a required literal, which is a property of its optimiser and not of the language.
- 2026-09-12 **An oracle expected-divergence list needs a discriminator recorded per row, and the control that proves it must be written first.** S33's `ExpectedDivergences` classifies a judged divergence as `expected` so a generator can go back on the default list; the first version's `search-start-partial` predicate was "a search, asked with partial, where upstream answered a partial and this port answered none", which **silently ate a mutation that reverted S33's own fix** - control S33-A reported 0 divergences over 2,000 rows at three seeds. The repair is `searchOnlyPartial`, recorded by `tools/record-oracle.py`: `search_start` is consulted on a search and nowhere else, so upstream's own `match` denying the partial its `search` reported at the same position is the family's signature and the matcher's own partial is not. Verified on six prefilter rows (all deny) and the real-bug row (agrees). **Rule: an entry without a recorded discriminator is a silencer, and the way to find out which you have is to write the negative control before the entry.**
- 2026-09-12 **Run every oracle generator at three seeds before believing a wave; one seed is how four families stayed hidden.** S33's blind review ran the generators at seeds no earlier slice had used and found that a default wave is not reliably green and was not before S33 either: forward `(*SKIP)` in a bounded repeat (`regex.finditer(r'(?:[^\d](*SKIP)){2,3}', '\r\naabb ', regex.M, overlapped=True)` - the port matches more than upstream), a reversed group call recording a capture whose end precedes its start (`spans('g') == [(3, 2), (1, 3)]`), and the bounded-lazy-repeat partial family, which the `run-oracle.ps1` note had said no generator reaches. None is caused by S33 - each reproduces on `fb4e705` - and `verbs` therefore stayed off the default list, because a generator whose rows red every later slice's run for a reason that is not that slice's is the thing S29 held it out to avoid. A 2000-row generator run costs about fifteen seconds.

Measured the same day: pin 2026.8.12, upstream at 2026.9.10 = head, 21 commits, five releases, the four 611-614 fixes plus Python-API error propagation. Issue 614's fix means upstream now agrees with this port on S30's group-call-in-lookbehind row, which the strict divergence list must notice at the sync.
- 2026-09-12 **Two of the three divergences S33 left for S34 are bugs UPSTREAM HAS ALREADY FIXED, and the way to find that out was to fetch upstream's history rather than read the pinned submodule.** `git -C upstream fetch` from this session showed five releases past the 2026.8.12 pin. - 2026-09-12 **This port still carries upstream's pre-613 `GREEDY_REPEAT_ONE` backtrack code, and that is a known inherited defect with a name, not a suspicion.** `Matcher.cs`'s GreedyRepeatOne arm clamps the retreat limit up to `SliceStart` and then stops only on `pos == limit`, which is upstream's code at the pin, verbatim. Upstream added a second clamp because a `(*SKIP)`-moved slice can raise the limit above `pos` and let a repeat unmatch below its minimum. No wave row reaches it here - every overlapped `(*SKIP)` answer this port gives matches upstream's own per-position `match` - so it is not fixed in S34: porting an upstream fix test-first is the sync slice's job, and inventing the test for it here would guess at the trigger. A divergence is then classified only when upstream's stateful scanner disagrees with that walk AND this port agrees with it, rendering for rendering. This is the analogue of S33's `searchOnlyPartial` and it is what let `verbs` join the default generator list: the alternative predicate, "a `(*SKIP)` scan whose two answers differ", also classifies a real defect in this port's own scan. The walk records whole matches and not just spans because a carried slice shows in a CAPTURE as readily as in a span - at seed 314159 upstream reports group 1 as (0, 4) for a match spanning (1, 4), a capture outside its own match in a pattern with no lookaround.

The sync must re-record every example.** `Every_expected_divergence_still_diverges` runs each example through the live engine and compares against the recorded upstream answer, which is what catches a port-side regression. It is blind to upstream fixing its side, because the recorded half never moves. Two entries are already waiting for exactly that. Written into the class remarks as a procedure, and the Phase 6 sync is its first test.
- 2026-09-12 **A wave counts at three seeds, and the default count of 300 rows per generator is not enough either.** `run-oracle.ps1` now takes `-Seeds` (comma-separated, because `pwsh -File` hands every argument over as a string and a real `[int[]]` binds the second seed to the next parameter) and defaults to 7, 4242 and the run's date, running each in turn and keeping a red seed's wave and report under its own name. The default list, all twenty generators including `verbs`, is green at those three seeds. At `-Count 2000` the same three seeds turn up one more row, and it is **a crash both engines share**: `regex.compile('(?r)^İﬁ', regex.I | regex.F)` raises `IndexError: tuple index out of range` from `String.get_firstset` (`_regex_core.py:4036`) on an empty `String` node, and this port raises `IndexOutOfRangeException` from the ported `String.GetFirstset` (`Nodes.cs:2099`) for the same reason. Not fixed in S34 - it is a new inherited defect with no upstream answer to port, so it is drafted upstream as report 6 and handed to Phase 6's issue sweep. **Read this as the rule generalising: a green wave is green at the count and seeds it ran, and nothing more.**

Re-verified the same day against 2026.9.10 in `.venvs/regex-2026.9.10`: every entry reproduces unchanged except the reversed group-call span (614's fix, now the port's answer); the `(?<=(?&a))c` row S30 pinned is **not** fixed by 614.

Roslyn 5.9.0-1.26379 CodeStyle. So the build gate for that rule is ReSharper's own engine, `jb inspectcode`, in CI and in `tools/check-inspections.ps1`, with the IDE0320 lines kept in `.editorconfig` for the day Roslyn reports it. CA1822 does enforce in the build and deliberately exempts test methods; TUnit0048 forbids static `[Test]` methods (a build error, verified), so the Rider "member can be made static" findings on test methods are disapplied for `tests/`.
- 2026-09-12 **401 non-capturing lambdas across 88 files made `static`, from ReSharper's inspection report, and the gate is `tools/check-inspections.ps1` in its own CI job.** The sites came from `jb inspectcode` (Roslyn IDE0320 running inside ReSharper's host, which does report it), applied by offset with a matcher that refused any range not starting on a lambda or delegate - one of 401 needed a hand fix, a discard-parameter lambda whose range began at `=>`. Ratchet GREEN before and after, 5578 passing either way.
