Close is S36. `regex` 2026.9.10 lives in `.venvs/regex-2026.9.10` (gitignored) because slice sessions cannot `pip install` - 26 allowlist denials in S34's log - but can run a Python that is already there.
- 2026-09-12 **S29's `search_start_END_OF_LINE_rev` verdict is REVERSED: the port was wrong, and `TryMatchEndOfLine` now reads `TextEnd` rather than `SliceEnd`.** Upstream's fast and slow paths do disagree about the bound, exactly as S29 measured - but `$` under MULTILINE is true at the end of the text and before a newline and nowhere else, so on `"\nb"` at position 1, where the next character is `b`, it is false whichever bound the code reads. It is the slow path that is wrong, on both sides, and this port had only the slow path. **A verb moves where the next attempt starts and nothing else** (PCRE2 pcre2pattern, "Verbs that act after backtracking"), so no assertion may read a bound a `(*SKIP)` has moved; every other zero-width assertion here already read the text bound, and the blind review re-checked all of them. The whole `search-start-skip-slice` family stopped diverging and its `ExpectedDivergences` entry is deleted - **the first time the staleness alarm has removed an entry rather than defended one, which is the argument for keeping the list strict.** Two rows of the family survived as a different mechanism, `overlapped-skip-stale-slice-reversed`.
- 2026-09-12 **`Sequence._fix_full_casefold` slices the unfolded run with folded offsets, and this port maps the chunk START back to a character boundary while leaving the END drifted.** Upstream's arithmetic works out for one expansion and fails for two: `ﬁaﬁ` stops matching `fiafi`, `ﬀaﬃ` stops matching `ffaffi`, and `(?r)^İﬁ` builds an empty `String` and crashes in `get_firstset`. Leaving the end alone is deliberate and measured - it drifts the same way, so it only ever takes in trailing characters that did not need the full fold, and doing so **keeps the emitted bytecode bit-identical to upstream's on every pattern upstream gets right**, where a both-ends mapping splits compile-parity corpus rows #323 and #333 differently for no behavioural gain. It costs a `pos >= characters.Count` guard, which the first attempt lacked: the blind review found 1,764 crashing runs of four characters that both minimised tests passed, and the sweep test over all 22,620 short runs is the guard against a third route.
- 2026-09-12 **The differential oracle cannot detect an inherited bug, demonstrated rather than asserted.** S35 wrote the negative control for the case-folding fix, reverted the fix, and ran `case-folding` at 600 rows on three seeds: **0 divergences**, because reverting it makes this port agree with upstream, which has the same bug. The control was deleted rather than kept, since one that can never fire is noise in every future run. The generator rows S35's slice file asked for were written and reverted for the mirror reason: after the fix they find 34 divergences of 600 at seed 7, every one of them upstream's bug, which would red the default wave permanently and need a classification entry whose only job is to hide it. **A gap test in the ordinary suite is the stronger and cheaper alarm for a bug both engines share**; the oracle's job is finding ours. ROADMAP's Phase 6 entry already said this in the abstract; these are the numbers.
- 2026-09-12 **Ledger entry 7: `İ` (U+0130) never reaches the full fold on either side, and it is left open on purpose.** `_fix_full_casefold` builds its expansion inventory with `fold_case` alone while the text it looks in is `fold_case(...).lower()`, and U+0130 is the one character the two disagree about, so no chunk is ever marked for it and `İ` does not match `i̇`. Lowering the inventory is one word and half-works: the matcher's `STRING_FLD` folds with the same `fold_case`, so U+0130 would have to expand there too - a change to the folding tables and to every construct that reads them. **This is the only known bug S35 identified and did not fix**; under the no-known-bugs rule it needs a slice before 1.0, and whether Phase 6's sweep takes it is an owner decision.
- 2026-09-12 **The three `verbs` rows S35 left are all upstream's, and the port is right on all three - including the one the S36 slice file called a port bug.** Row 1567's damning evidence was that this port's own `search(0, 14)` finds the match its overlapped scan does not; but `search(0, 14)` truncates the subject, which is what makes `$` true there, and inside the scan the character at 14 is an `A`. Minimised to `regex.finditer(r'(?r)(?:.{2}(*SKIP)A|x)$', 'bxA', regex.M, overlapped=True)` = `[(0, 3), (1, 2)]` against this port's `[(0, 3)]`, with two controls - delete the verb, or make it `(*PRUNE)` - each leaving upstream with `[(0, 3)]` alone. So it is S35's defect seen through a scan, and the strongest evidence yet that S35's reversal of S29 was right. **Row 1439 does NOT depend on call order**, which is what the slice file suspected from the wave and the isolated run disagreeing: `verbs` is recorded with `locate_required_string` neutralised and the isolated run was not. Recompiled prefilter-free it is deterministic. The recorder therefore needs no per-row isolation, and that non-change is recorded as loudly as a change would have been, because "upstream's answer depends on call order" would have been a standing hazard for every later wave.
- 2026-09-12 **`reverse-group-call-direction` is renamed `group-call-direction` and widened, because issue 614 is about the DIRECTION and not about `(?r)`.** A called group runs the wrong way round whenever the lookaround containing the call runs the other way from the pattern, so a LOOKBEHIND in an ordinary forward pattern has it too: `regex.compile(r'(?P<g1>A*)(?<=(?&g1))').finditer('A')` records g1's second capture at `(2, 1)` on a one-character subject - a start past the end, an end before its own start - where 2026.9.10 and this port both answer `(0, 1)`. Found by the composed `interactions` wave at seed 7, which is the first generator to put a call inside a lookaround at all. **The same row broke the recorder**: `_to_index_length` indexed a two-entry codepoint table with 2 and raised `IndexError`, so one upstream bug failed a whole 2000-row wave. `_utf16_index` now EXTENDS an out-of-subject index rather than clamping it - one unit per codepoint past the end, a negative index kept - so an impossible span stays impossible on this side and is reported rather than quietly made plausible; the recorder's `--self-check` gained two cases, watched failing against a clamping version.
- 2026-09-12 **A row count is a seed by another name, and the `interactions` widening is bounded at 2000 rows rather than claimed green at 6000.** The composed generator is green at the default 300 and at 2000 on three seeds, and RED at 6000 on five - 13 rows, every one of them in a family already judged: 9 are S30's group call inside an opposite-direction lookaround, 3 are `search_start`'s own partial arms in a shape `search-start-partial`'s predicate does not reach, and 1 is a new `bounded-lazy-repeat-partial` row. Classifying them is a slice's work and is handed on rather than rushed. **Two shortcuts were tried, measured and reverted**: recording `interactions` prefilter-free removed one diverging row and introduced another (those three rows are `search_start`, which Python cannot reach, not `locate_required_string`, which it can); and retargeting five thin controls at the composed generator made all five fire at every seed until the unmutated baseline was measured, at which point every figure was at or below it.
- 2026-09-12 **Phase 4 closes at ten slices and eleven sessions, 1.1 against Phase 3's 1.0, with a median of 50.9M tokens.** The three slices beyond the seven authored were not scope creep and not features: each came from verification getting stricter (act on the divergence research, make three seeds the floor, fix what the wider wave then exposed) and each found a real defect. `run-controls.py` gained `--seeds N` because a control accumulates seeds and a mutated engine is often slow as well as wrong - S28-C's 2400-row wave takes ten minutes under its own mutation against 44 seconds clean. Of the 24 Phase 4 controls re-run at their recorded seed and at a fresh 99991, 20 are healthy and 4 are thin or dead (S28-E 0/0, S29-A 0/1, S29-D 2/0, S35-A 0/0) - and all four were already recorded as weak by the slice that wrote them, so the re-run corroborated the notes rather than surprising anyone.
- 2026-09-12 **`ENHANCEMATCH`'s dead `same_match` check is not ported as live code** (S41). Upstream computes `same_match` and overrides it to `FALSE` on the next line (`_regex.c:17942-17943`), so its `same_span_of_group` loop never runs. Bisected against PyPI source releases: 2014.12.24 and 2015.09.28 had the check live (`if (same) break;`) in one combined best/enhanced loop; 2015.11.5 (Hg issue 165, "Performance / hung search") split the loop into `do_simple`/`do_enhanced`/`do_best` and introduced `same_match` already overridden. The check is only an earlier exit: honouring it can never find a match the current code misses and can lose one, and the override costs exactly one failing run per enhanced match on an already-narrowed slice. Verdict: deliberate, not a bug. Port the effective behaviour, keep the dead lines as a quoted comment, and put `same_span_of_group` in PORTMAP's deliberately-not-ported table. S41 measures it (honoured check behind a temporary switch, wave both ways, runs counted).
- 2026-09-12 **`BESTMATCH` and `ENHANCEMATCH` rank by cost in this port, ties by fewer errors, then earliest** (owner decision; S41, S42). Upstream ranks by error count only (`total_errors`, never `total_cost`), which is open issue 470: `(?b)(voices){1i+1d+2s<=2}` on `voixes voicees` returns the cost-2 substitution over the cost-1 insertion because both are one error - verified on 2026.9.10, `tools/probes/upstream-bestmatch-cost-ranking.py`. Evidence it is a regression: releases up to 2015.09.28 ranked by cost (`state->max_cost = state->total_cost - 1`) and the 2015.11.5 rework replaced `max_cost` with `max_errors` throughout; TRE/agrep, the only other engine with per-type costs, defines best as lowest cost; upstream's docs define costs but never define "best"; its one weighted `(?b)` test answers the same under both rules. With unit costs the rules agree, so no ported test changes; the divergence is confined to cost equations under `(?b)`/`(?e)`, pinned by gap tests and a strict `ExpectedDivergences` predicate, and ledgered (nothing filed). Procedure in both slices: port upstream's rule first and get the tag green, then switch through one shared helper.
- 2026-09-12 **Phase 5 authored as S37-S43** and approved by the owner the same day; the split and its reasons are in ROADMAP's Phase 5 note.
- 2026-09-12 **`interactions` is green at 6000 rows on four seeds, and S36's thirteen rows were three families, not the three it named.** S36's table said 9 group-call rows, 3 `(*SKIP)`-plus-partial and 1 `bounded-lazy-repeat-partial`. At the three default seeds the rows are 4 group-call, 3 partial and **one reversed overlapped `(*SKIP)` extra match** - no bounded-lazy row appeared at all - which is what a different seed set does to a per-family count and why the families rather than the counts are what was carried forward. Three changes closed them: a new entry `group-call-loses-the-match`; `search-start-partial` widened to the arm where this port answers its OWN partial elsewhere; and `CarriesACaptureOutsideItself` narrowed from "any lookaround" to "any POSITIVE lookaround". None needed engine work, which is what the slice predicted.
- 2026-09-12 **A negative lookaround cannot put a capture outside a match, so only the positive forms and `\K` are excluded from the capture tell.** `regex.search(r'(a)(?!(?:(b))x)b', 'ab')` is (0, 2) with group 1 `['a']` and group 2 EMPTY, and this port agrees; a negative lookaround only succeeds when its body fails, so nothing it matched survives. The old clause excluded `(?!` and `(?<!` as well and kept one real row of `overlapped-skip-extra-match-reversed` out of its own entry for no reason. `tools/probes/upstream-negative-lookaround-captures.py`.
- 2026-09-12 **Upstream loses a match when a group call sits inside a lookaround running the other way, and the judgement needs no second engine: deleting the piece that holds the call, which in every judged row can match zero-width, gives upstream the match it refused.** Five rows in four outcome shapes, all unchanged on 2026.9.10, so issue 614's fix does not cover it (ledger entry 8). **The minimal form is NOT established and the entry says so**: every shrink that kept upstream self-contradictory landed on a pattern this port answers the same way upstream does, so "a call through an opposite-direction lookaround" is necessary and not sufficient, and the rows are listed whole.
- 2026-09-12 **`search-start-partial`'s second symptom is keyed on ROWS, not on a predicate, and the blind review is why.** The first draft asked only that UPSTREAM's partial cover the whole searched region - genuinely `search_start`'s fingerprint - and put no condition on this port's answer beyond "a partial, rendered differently". One added line in `Matcher.cs`'s partial block (`state.MatchPos = state.Reverse ? state.MatchPos + 1 : state.MatchPos - 1;`) then had the entry's own minimised row classified EXPECTED with the port answering (3, 1) instead of (4, 0). Row-keyed it is `expected 0 diverge 4` mutated and `expected 4 diverge 0` clean. **The general rule this is the second instance of** (after `bounded-lazy-repeat-partial`): when a family's discriminator is a fact about UPSTREAM alone, a predicate cannot be strict, because strictness in this list comes from pinning what THIS PORT says.
- 2026-09-12 **`CarriesACaptureOutsideItself` reads the pattern with every space removed, because `(?x)(?<  =(a))` is a positive lookbehind.** Upstream reads the character after `(?<` with `source.get()` rather than `get(True)` (`_regex_core.py:863`), so VERBOSE whitespace is skipped there and the spaced spelling captures outside the match exactly as `(?<=` does. The blind review built the two rows, applied control S37-C and watched the spaced one classified while its twin was reported. Stripping is conservative in the safe direction: it can only add an exclusion, and an exclusion only reddens the run.
- 2026-09-12 **A fourth seed at 6000 rows found a row three seeds did not, and the predicate's deliberate gap is what caught it.** `UpstreamFoundStrictlyLess` was written with no `split` arm, on the rule that a shape no row has shown should red the run rather than fall into an untested predicate; seed 99991 then drew a `split` row of exactly that family. This is evidence for the VERIFICATION rule 7a floor rather than a new rule: three seeds stays the floor, and a slice widening a generator should say which seeds it used.
- 2026-09-13 **`match_fuzzy_changes` reports only the first `Total` entries of the change list, not the whole list.** Upstream's loop is `for (i = 0; i < count; i++)` where `count` is the sum of the three fuzzy COUNTS (`_regex.c:20522`), not the length of the array `pattern_new_match` copied (`:20761-20770`). A nested fuzzy section leaves the two out of step - `END_FUZZY` recomputes the counts and does not always unwind the change list to match - so the counts and the changes can genuinely disagree about which KIND of error happened, and upstream reports them disagreeing. Reading the whole list instead put an extra change on four rows across the three default seeds of S38's `fuzzy` wave. Upstream has no bound check there at all, so its `count` can exceed its own array; the port clamps. Pinned by `FuzzyMatchingTests.Only_as_many_changes_are_reported_as_the_counts_say_even_when_more_were_recorded`.
- 2026-09-13 **`start_match`'s fuzzy-counts clear (`_regex.c:11790-11792`) is load-bearing, and only a backtracking verb makes it visible.** Ordinarily the `FUZZY` backtrack arm has already restored the counts by the time `FAILURE` jumps back to `start_match`, so the clear looks redundant - which is why S38's first draft left it out. `(*PRUNE)` and `(*SKIP)` cut the backtracking and drop the fuzzy frames instead of unwinding them, and then the next start position begins with the abandoned attempt's errors: `regex.search(r'(?:[ab][bc](*PRUNE)[wx]){e<=2}', 'qab')` is `(1, 3) (0, 0, 1)` upstream and was `(2, 0, 1)` here. Found by S38's blind review; the `fuzzy` generator emits no verbs and could not have.
- 2026-09-13 **`state->fuzzy_guards` is never to be ported, on the same evidence as `group_call_guard_list`.** `grep -n fuzzy_guards upstream/src/_regex.c` (2026-09-13) gives a declaration, a null, two `reset_guard_list` calls in `reset_guards`, an allocation, a `memset` and the frees - twelve lines, no read. So a fuzzy section's body and tail guards guard nothing. The holes S16 left in `MatchState.Create` and `ResetGuards` said "waits for Phase 5"; they now say "never", with the grep and the date. `RE_FuzzyData.limit` is the same shape (written at `:10203` and `:10206`, read nowhere) and is left out of `Engine.FuzzyData`.
- 2026-09-13 **A fuzzy section of two adjacent literals is a fuzzy STRING, so `(?:foo){e<=1}` is not in the one-character subset.** `Sequence.pack_characters` (`_regex_core.py:3526`) packs a run of two or more positive, non-zero-width `Character` items into one `STRING` node; anything else - a class, a property, a dot, an assertion - flushes the run. S38's first draft of both the gap tests and the oracle generator used word-like literals throughout and every row hit the STRING seam. Both were rewritten to keep literals apart, and the rule is written at the top of `FuzzyMatchingTests` and beside `FUZZY_ATOMS` in `tools/record-oracle.py`.
- 2026-09-13 **`any_error_permitted` cannot be shown to matter in the simple fuzzy path, and that is a fact about the predicate rather than about the generator.** Its cost test is strictly weaker than `this_error_permitted`'s (`cost + this_cost <= MAX_COST`, with every per-error cost at least 1) and its `error_count < max_errors` test is dead where `do_simple_fuzzy_match` sets `max_errors` to `PY_SSIZE_T_MAX`. Two mutations of it - relaxing the cost gate by one and tightening it to `<` - found nothing at either seed; only "the predicate is consulted at all" is observable, which is what control S38-A now measures (282 and 265 of 600). Expect the `max_errors` term to come alive in S41 and S42.
- 2026-09-13 **A `stackalloc` inside a `switch` case inside the matcher's loops is unbounded stack growth, and CA2014 does not see it.** Stackalloc memory lives until the method returns, so one per backtrack step accumulates. S38's first draft had three inside the `END_FUZZY` cases; the analyzer passed them and fired only on a control mutation that put one directly in a case. They are now declared once beside `folded`/`gfolded` at the top of `BasicMatch`, which is the convention that was already there. The rule for the engine is: no `stackalloc` below the `advance:`/`backtrack:` labels, whatever the analyzer says.
- 2026-09-13 **A control that fires at no seed is a finding, and the finding has to be reproduced by hand before it is believed.** S38's control E (the nested section inheriting the outer counts) is silent at both seeds because the generator's sections are too short for the outer one to have spent an error before the inner is entered - not because the fault is unobservable. `tools/probes/fuzzy-nested-rows.py` builds 24 rows that do reach it: 24 agree on the honest engine, 3 agree and 21 diverge with the mutation. The shapes are pinned as a test, and the control stays in `controls.json` with its zero recorded, because a zero with an explanation is evidence and a zero without one is a gap.
- 2026-09-13 **The fuzzy branches of both `*_REPEAT_ONE` backtrack arms are unreachable, so S39 ports neither.** `sequence_matches_one` (`_regex.c:24056`) refuses a `REPEAT_ONE` whose body node carries `RE_STATUS_FUZZY`, and every op `node_matches_one_character` (`:3433`) accepts comes from a parser class that sets `FUZZY_OP` inside a section (`Any`, `Character`, `Property`, `Range`, `SetBase`, `SetUnion`, `ZeroWidthBase`), so no `REPEAT_ONE` exists inside one; outside one, the node after the repeat is `FUZZY` or `FUZZY_EXT`, which `Fuzzy._compile` emits with `REVERSE_OP` only, and `can_test_past` (`:23697`) walks past neither. Measured over all 1,534 compile-parity patterns: 639 `REPEAT_ONE` nodes, none with a fuzzy test node. The greedy branch would have been a copy of its own default arm anyway. `RepeatTests.No_repeat_one_node_in_the_corpus_has_a_fuzzy_test_node` is the guard, not the claim: it reds if a sync changes any of the three facts. Found because the two gap tests written for those loops passed before S39 wrote a line.
- 2026-09-13 **`string_pos` is an index into a node's values for a `STRING*` and a subject position for a `REF_GROUP*`, and this port has to say which.** Upstream's `fuzzy_match_string` moves it with `data->new_string_pos += step` for both, because a C position is a codepoint count either way; here one moves by `+ step` and the other by `Matcher.Step`, so `FuzzyData.StringPosIsText` exists with no upstream counterpart. The S21 comment at `REF_GROUP`'s own walk predicted exactly this and kept `NextPos` for it.
- 2026-09-13 **S35's `_fix_full_casefold` divergence is reachable by an oracle wave, and the `fuzzy` generator deliberately stays off it.** Two expanding folds in one literal run make upstream drop the `FULLIGNORECASE` flag from the second chunk - `(?fi)ßaß` stops matching `'ssass'` there and matches here - and S39's widened generator is the first instrument to draw the shape: 22 divergences over three 2000-row seeds, every one this family. It is a COMPILE-time difference both sides' own tests already pin, and no predicate over the two answers is narrow enough for `ExpectedDivergences` because upstream compiles a genuinely different pattern. So the generator draws at most one expanding fold per section and **Phase 6 decides whether the manifest gets an entry**; hiding it behind a broad predicate would have blinded the wave to the matcher arms S39 ports.
- 2026-09-13 **An unbounded `{e}` budget is only safe over one-character atoms.** Next to a string, a fold, a repeat or a backreference it makes upstream raise `MemoryError`, which kills the recorder and so the whole wave - `regex.sub(r'(abx)(?:\W[a-f](?:[ab]+\1){e}){s<=2}', '<>', 'abx.dabx')` and `regex.split(r'(?fi)(?:straße\d\A){e}', 'sTrasßE0')` on 2026.7.19, the same family as upstream issues 551 and 554. `FUZZY_BOUNDED_CONSTRAINTS` is what a section with a multi-character item draws from; the generator stays off the shape rather than the recorder learning to survive it.
- 2026-09-13 **`tools/run-controls.py` caches waves by generator, count and seed, and the key does not know the generator changed.** S39's first control run read zero for all six controls at seed 7 and non-zero at the new seed, because `.scratch/control-waves/fuzzy-600-7.jsonl` was still S38's wave - a generator with no strings, folds or backreferences in it. **Delete the cached wave for any generator you have widened**, or the controls measure a generator that no longer exists. This is the same trap the port-slice skill records for figures recorded mid-slice, arriving by a different door.
- 2026-09-13 **A `needs:` tag names the capability a test waits on, not the feature it is about.** With S40's `{...:test}` constraint ported, 31 test cases tagged `fuzzy-matching`, `fuzzy-budget`, `fuzzy-counts` or `fuzzy-changes` were still red, and **all 31 failed at the engine's own `ENHANCEMATCH` or `BESTMATCH` seam rather than on an assertion** - their patterns carry `(?e)`, `(?b)`, `(?be)` or `FuzzyRegexOptions.BestMatch`. They are retagged to the two capabilities that actually gate them, and eight upstream methods mixing plain and ranking rows in one `[Arguments]` fan-out were split so a plain row is not skipped to keep a `(?e)` sibling company. The slice rule against retagging a red test is against retagging *instead of* analysing it; the analysis here is the seam's own exception message, which is mechanical. Design spec amendment 18, and the ROADMAP's Phase 5 paragraph.
- 2026-09-13 **`fuzzy_ext_match` and `fuzzy_ext_match_group_fld` are asymmetric in upstream, and both asymmetries are ported as written.** `fuzzy_ext_match` (`_regex.c:9938`) lists `SET_*` and `SET_*_IGN` but no `SET_*_REV` or `SET_*_IGN_REV`; `fuzzy_ext_match_group_fld` (`:10033`) lists no `SET_*_IGN` either. An opcode with no arm falls off the end of the switch to `return TRUE`, so a reversed set test - and, inside a case folding, any set test at all - constrains nothing where the same test forward would bite. Measured on regex 2026.7.19: `(?<=(?:[ab][cd]){e<=1:[0-9x]})$` against `'azc'` substitutes where `{e<=1:x}` has to delete instead. `.` is a no-op everywhere for the same reason, ANY having no arm at all. `Gaps/Engine/FuzzyTestConstraintTests.cs` pins each one; whether upstream should be told is Phase 6's, not this slice's.
- 2026-09-13 **A fuzzy test accepts only a character set, which makes three ports of upstream code unreachable and one V1-only.** `{e<=1:(?-i:x)}` and `{e<=1:(?i)x}` are both `error: expected character set`, so a test compiled under `(?fi)` is always an `_IGN` opcode and `fuzzy_ext_match_group_fld`'s non-`_IGN` arms cannot run; they are ported because upstream writes them. `SET_DIFF`, `SET_INTER` and `SET_SYM_DIFF` need the set-operator syntax, which needs `(?V1)`, so the oracle generator - which emits V0 patterns - cannot reach them and `FuzzyTestConstraintTests.The_other_three_set_opcodes_reach_the_same_arm` covers them directly instead.
- 2026-09-13 **A control can be blind because no *test* in the pool discriminates, not because the code path is cold.** S40's control E replaces `folded_char_at`'s `folded_pos` with a literal `0` and read 0 of 2000 at both seeds three times running. The path was live all along - inverting the same arm's verdict diverged 1 and 10 rows - but the foldings the generator produces are `ß`->"ss" and `ﬀ`->"ff", two identical characters, and every one of the 15 tests in the pool answered the same for both halves of every folding. `FUZZY_SPLIT_TESTS` exists for that: `[a-s]` splits "st", `[a-f]` splits "fi". **When a control reads zero, ask whether the instrument can tell the two answers apart before concluding the shape is unreachable** - that is a third failure mode alongside "the generator never draws the shape" (S38's control E) and "the cached wave is the old generator" (S39's).
- 2026-09-13 **A row upstream never finishes is a recorded outcome, not a dead wave.** `tools/record-oracle.py` gives every upstream call `timeout=10` and records a miss as a kind of its own, `{"kind": "timeout", "seconds": N}`; the consumer reads it as `TimeoutOutcome`, skips the row without asking this port - there is no ground truth to compare against, and asking would spend `OracleComparer.RowTimeout` per row for nothing - and tallies it under `OracleVerdict.Timeout` so the report's summary line shows it. **A kind of its own rather than an `error`**, because upstream did not reject the pattern: filing it as a rejection would score a port that answers as diverging and a port that also hangs as agreeing, which is both halves of the comparison backwards. Ten seconds to match `RowTimeout` - neither engine gets longer than the other to answer the same question - and the recorded value is that CONSTANT, never an elapsed time, which is what keeps `--verify-determinism` sound. It fires for real: 6000 rows a generator at seed 4242 records `timeout 1`.
- 2026-09-13 **The slice goes back to what the caller asked for at the start of every match, and that is a deliberate departure from upstream's code.** `(*SKIP)` moves `slice_start`/`slice_end` mid-attempt (`_regex.c:14553`) and upstream restores them nowhere, so one scanner state carried a moved slice into the next match - upstream's own bug, ledger entry 5, whose proposed fix is exactly this reset. This port could not simply inherit it: with no `search_start` prefilter until Phase 7 it reaches the carried slice on shapes upstream's optimiser skips past, and there it LOSES MATCHES rather than merely moving them (`finditer(r'\b(?:[^a](*SKIP))*', 'b\n\rS', overlapped=True)` was three matches here and four upstream, and this port's own `Match` at the missing position found it). `Matcher.DoMatch` resets from `MatchState.InitialSliceStart`/`InitialSliceEnd`, and **`FuzzyRegex.NewMatch` records those too** - handing `Match.NextMatch` the moved slice reintroduced the defect on that one path, which S40a's blind review caught with a scan and a walk disagreeing.
- 2026-09-13 **A fuzzy match can report changes that contradict its own counts, and that is upstream's, not ours.** `basic_match`'s `start_match` clears `fuzzy_counts` and leaves `fuzzy_changes` (`:11790`), and `match_fuzzy_changes` reports the first `sum(counts)` entries (`:20522`), so a change recorded by an abandoned attempt DISPLACES the winning attempt's. Upstream's own `search(r'(?:[ab][bc](*PRUNE)[wx]){e<=2}', 'qab')` is `counts=(0,0,1)` with a SUBSTITUTION at 0, unchanged on 2026.9.10, and S38 had already pinned both rows. **The one-line fix - clearing the list beside the counts - was made, measured and reverted**: it turns those pinned rows red and makes this port diverge where it currently agrees. Ledger entry 11; an inherited bug, so by the owner's 2026-09-12 rule it is fixed here before 1.0, in Phase 6's sweep beside entry 7. The lookbehind rows S40's review read as a port bug differ only because `$` lets upstream's prefilter make one attempt where this port makes four.
- 2026-09-13 **Restoring the slice before `DoMatch`'s partial retry is part-right and is NOT done.** The two `DoMatch2` calls are two attempts at one match, so a `(*SKIP)` in the non-partial pass leaves `slice_start` moved for the partial pass and the search stops being leftmost - a defect on this port's own answers (`\b\D(*SKIP)z` over `' A'`: search (2,0), this port's own `MatchAtStart` at 1 gives (1,1), and so does upstream). Adding the restore fixes that row, **introduces** a reversed one - restoring `slice_end` moves what every end-of-subject assertion means - and turns S37's permanent `A_skip_alternation_partial_starts_where_this_port_ran_out_of_text` red, which turns out to be the same defect pinning (4,0) where this port's own matcher answers (2,2) earlier. All three want judging together, in a slice of their own. Written out at the code so it is not rediscovered.
- 2026-09-13 **The 6000-row gate at three seeds finds fifteen diverging rows, not four.** S40 recorded four because only seed 7 ever completed; 4242 gives five and 20260913 gives seven, eleven of them never triaged, and **one is a crash** - row 93133 throws `ArgumentException: capture index out of range` out of `Substitution.ExpandField` where upstream answers `sub 0`. Two independent blind reviews confirmed none of the fifteen is caused by S40a's diff (the same `wave.jsonl` consumed against a reverted engine gives a byte-identical report). So "the default wave green at 6000 rows" is not a slice-sized exit gate, and S40a's closing section proposes splitting it four ways with the crash first. This is the Phase 4 pattern again: verification got stricter and found real defects.
- 2026-09-13 **Row 93133 is not a crash, and the entry above it is wrong about that - this supersedes it.** `Substitution.ExpandField`'s `ArgumentException: capture index out of range` is exactly what upstream raises for the identical template the moment it has a match to expand it against: `{1[2]}` names a third capture of a group that made one, and deleting the lookahead gives upstream a match and then `IndexError: list index out of range`. The port's format-field handling was measured against upstream's on seven index forms - `{0}`, `{0[0]}`, `{0[-1]}`, `{0[-2]}`, `{1[0]}`, `{1[2]}`, `{2[-1]}` - and agrees on every one, negative indices included. So the whole divergence is upstream LOSING THE MATCH, which is `group-call-loses-the-match`, and `UpstreamFoundStrictlyLess` now has an arm for a substitution row whose port-side answer is a rejection rather than a replacement, narrowed to `ArgumentException` with its hole named. Re-runnable: the last two sections of `tools/probes/upstream-group-call-loses-matches.py`, which print the row and then upstream's answer to each of the seven index forms beside this port's recorded one.
- 2026-09-13 **The operation was never what discriminated `overlapped-skip-extra-match-reversed`, so it now covers a plain reversed `finditer` too.** The prefix comparison and the per-extra-match refutation are what keep it narrow, and neither was loosened. The row that forced it is this port's own improvement: with the per-match slice reset in, a plain reversed scan stops carrying the moved slice, so seed 20260913 row 116766 - `(?r)[[:digit:]]*(*SKIP)a$` over `'\r\nAAa\r\naaa'` - now ends at upstream's first match where it used to reproduce all three, and upstream's own `(?m)$` holds at 1, 6 and 10 alone while its second and third matches need it where the subject has an `a`. **Two rows of the same family are deliberately left red**: a `sub` row carries no match positions for a tell to read, and an overlapped row with no `$` and no groups has no tell at all - its refutation is upstream's own search over a truncated subject, which is the reversed `anchoredScan` the recorder refuses. `tools/probes/upstream-reversed-skip-scan-shapes.py` runs all three.
- 2026-09-13 **Upstream leaks a partial through a group call, on both widths and in 2026.9.10, and the odd answer on this side is the ASCII one - not the astral one S40a first named.** A call inside an opposite-direction lookaround with an OPTIONAL tail reports `partial=True` upstream where the same lookaround written out reports a complete match; that is ledger entry 8's signature and it survives issue 614's fix, so the sync will not remove it. Across twelve cases this port answers "not partial" for every optional tail and "partial" for every required one, at both widths - except the two ASCII CALL rows, `(?P<g1>A)(?:(?<=(?P>g1))\w)?` over `'A'` and its reversed twin `(?r)(?P<g1>\w+)(?:(?!(?P>g1))\s)?` over `'a'`, where it reports upstream's partial. So the question for S40c is why those two leak, not why the astral rows do not, and the answer decides whether seven wave rows become one `ExpectedDivergences` entry or none. **Five of the seven are "upstream partial, this port a complete match of the same span" and two are not**: row 98191's subject holds no astral character and this port answers no match at all, and row 74396 is a substitution where neither engine reports a partial and the replacement counts differ - so the astral subject is what the generators draw, not the precondition. `tools/probes/upstream-call-partial-leak.py`, `--newer` for 2026.9.10.
- 2026-09-13 **A green commit that leaves the slice file pending is a CHECKPOINT, not a failure.** `Get-SliceFailureReason` returns the literal `checkpoint` when the session committed, left the tree clean and the ratchet green but did not move its slice file; the driver keeps the commit, logs `checkpoint`, resets the failure count and starts a fresh session on the same slice, stopping after three. Before this the driver rolled such commits back and auto-retried from scratch: S29 (2026-09-11) and S40a (2026-09-13) both ended a session that way on purpose, both were rolled back, and each needed a recovery session that fished the commit out of the reflog (S40a's cost 40M tokens before it could start its own work). The ratchet check now runs before the pending check, so a red pending tree is still a failure. Pester tests cover both orderings.
- 2026-09-13 **The slice IS restored before `DoMatch`'s partial retry, at both ends - this supersedes the "part-right and NOT done" entry above.** That entry's blocking premise was measured and is backwards. It read the reversed row as a regression restoring `slice_end` introduces; the row is **upstream's own instance of the same defect**, in the direction its `search_start` prefilter does not mask. A reversed search is anchored by its end and tries the highest `endpos` first, so the answer it owes is the first anchor that matches: upstream's search gives the zero-width partial at (0, 0) - the *last* anchor it would try - while its own `match(0, 1, partial=True)` gives (0, 1), and so does the same pattern with `(*PRUNE)`, which prunes backtracking identically and moves no bound. Three such rows are the `partial-retry-reversed-slice` entry (seeds 7 and 20260913, none at 4242). S37's pinned row was the same defect too and its answer moved from (4, 0) to (2, 2), which is upstream's own `match(pos=2)`, **narrowing `search-start-partial` to the prefilter alone**: a row whose only difference is where this port started is now a defect to fix, not a row to classify. What decided every part of it was self-refutation - a search that reports a position its own anchored matcher beats is wrong whatever upstream says - which is how the reversed half could be judged against an upstream that disagrees with it. Also a units trap worth naming: S40a's "(2, 2)" for the S37 row is index and length, and reads (2, 4) as a span; it was nearly filed as a correction.
- 2026-09-13 **The gate's three seeds cannot always separate a half-fix from a fix, and a fourth seed is the cheap way to find out.** S40b's control B - restoring the forward slice end only, leaving `slice_end` alone - scored **identically to the committed code** at seeds 7, 4242 and 20260913 (1+2+2 diverging on `partial,verbs` at 6000 rows). Only the classification moved, not the count, because **upstream shares the reversed defect and a port that shares it too simply AGREES**. Two things did separate them: this port's own self-consistency (the permanent test and the staleness alarm both red under B), and **seed 31**, where B leaves 4 diverging rows where the fix leaves 3 and the row it misses is reversed - restoring `slice_end` makes this port agree with upstream there. So when a control's number does not move, the instrument may be the thing that cannot see the difference, not the change; try a seed the gate does not use before concluding the two designs are equivalent. (Seed 31 also carries three unjudged divergences of its own, present with and without S40b's fix, recorded in S40b's closing notes for S40d.)
- 2026-09-13 **An oracle row index is meaningless without the command that drew it.** The index counts across every generator in a run, so "row 101560" identifies a row only under `tools/run-oracle.ps1 -Count 6000` with the full default generator list - 126,000 rows a seed. S40b's blind review looked for that slice's three rows in a 12,000-row `-Generator partial,verbs` wave, found them at 5560, 397 and 3556, and reported the recorded provenance as wrong. Nothing depends on the index, because every row-keyed `ExpectedDivergences` entry matches on the row's own question; it is a pointer for a reader, and it has to name the command to be one.
- 2026-09-13 **There is no group-call partial leak, and the entry above is superseded: upstream's extra partial is `min_width` showing through the two-pass structure, and the odd answers were this port's astral ones.** `do_match` answers a partial request with a NON-PARTIAL pass first and falls back only if it FAILS, and `do_exact_match`'s width early-out is guarded by `partial_side == RE_PARTIAL_NONE` (`_regex.c:18069`), so it fires on the first pass alone. `min_width` counts a group CALL at the callee's width even inside a zero-width LOOKAROUND, so `(?P<g1>A)(?:(?<=(?P>g1))\w)?` has `min_width` 2 against the inlined form's 1; on a one-character subject the non-partial pass is skipped on arithmetic and the partial pass answers instead. **It therefore depends on how many characters are AVAILABLE, not on the call**: more text removes the partial, and a wider callee moves the threshold by exactly the callee's width. Predicted before it was believed and confirmed nine for nine on 2026.7.19 and 2026.9.10 (`tools/probes/upstream-min-width-partial-retry.py`). **This port's defect was counting `available` in UTF-16 code units** - one astral character read as two, the early-out did not fire, the non-partial pass SUCCEEDED, and the retry never happened. The comment that had stood at that line, and its twin in `Substitution.cs`, argued the mismatch was safe because over-counting can only fail to fire and the engine then "does the work and fails in the dispatch loop instead - the same answer"; **a pass that succeeds is not a pass that fails**, and what it suppressed was the fallback. Five of S40c's seven wave rows now agree; row 98191 turned out to be `bounded-lazy-repeat-partial` and row 74396 `group-call-loses-the-match`, and one example row left the latter because the fix made it agree - its divergence had been the width early-out all along, which padding the subject proves.
- 2026-09-13 **A matrix that holds the deciding variable fixed cannot answer the question, however many cases it has.** S40a's twelve-case call/partial matrix varied the pattern's shape and never varied the subject's length, which is the only thing the early-out reads - so two sessions read it, drew opposite conclusions, and neither was right. What answered it in minutes was an env-gated opcode trace in `Matcher`'s two dispatch loops printing `partial_side`: the ASCII case ran one pass with it SET and the astral case one pass with it CLEAR, which says the two took different roads before matching began. **When a matrix is consistent and still does not explain, suspect the axis it does not have.** A related trap from the same slice: the first draft of the upstream prediction probe scored eight of nine, and the failing row was a badly chosen experiment rather than a refutation - its optional tail could actually match, so no partial could exist whatever the early-out did. Rebuilt with a `\w*` prefix so the tail stays past the end of the subject while the width varies, it scored nine of nine; the note is kept in the probe.
- 2026-09-13 **The recorder's reversed `anchoredScan` refusal was about the PATTERN, not about reversal, and it now reads the pattern.** `_anchored_scan` records upstream's own answer to an overlapped `(*SKIP)` scan asked one match at a time; it refused every reversed row because stepping one moves `endpos`, which truncates the subject and changes what `$`, `\Z`, `\b`, `\B`, `\m` and `\M` mean. A reversed pattern holding none of those can be walked, and the step is upstream's own - `state->text_pos = state->match_pos - 1` (`:20903`), where a reversed attempt's `match_pos` is the match's END. Measured on seven shapes including a zero-width one, all SAME, and on four end-reading shapes, all DIFFER: `python tools/probes/upstream-reversed-walk-step.py`. `_reads_the_end_of_the_subject` is a crude whitespace-stripped substring test over `$ \Z \z \b \B \m \M \K \G \X (?= (?!` and every way it is crude refuses MORE than it must, which can only cost a classification. It classified seed 4242 row 117071 and nothing else across 378,000 rows.
- 2026-09-13 **A diverging `sub` row carries no positions, so the recorder now records where upstream replaced.** `subMatches`, on a `sub`/`subf` row whose pattern holds a `(*SKIP)`, asked as a separate `finditer` because `subn` walks the same scanner - which makes the `$` tell readable on a substitution and classified seed 20260913 row 116388. The consumer refuses to read the list unless its length equals the recorded replacement count, and that guard immediately earned its place: the first draft truncated by the row's raw `count`, and **upstream's count convention is not a slice** - 0 means no limit and a NEGATIVE count replaces nothing, so a row upstream never touched was recorded with every match but the last. Found by the blind review, 8 bad rows in a 6000-row seed-7 `verbs` wave, 0 after the fix. The arm compares counts and not text, because a sub renders as one string and this port's own replacement spans are not in the row; the hole is stated beside the code.
- 2026-09-13 **The reversed carried slice has one more symptom - upstream's scanner LOSING a match, ledger entry 5's fourth - and a seed outside the gate's three found it in under a minute.** Seed 20260914, `verbs` row 3679: `(?r)\p{Lu}*(*SKIP)B(?P<g1>(?:\D{2,4}(*SKIP)a|.))` over `'B_\ra'`, upstream reporting one match where this port reports two. Same mechanism as ledger entry 5 with the moved `slice_end` too far LEFT rather than too far right, so the next attempt runs in a view that cannot hold the match. Upstream's own stepwise door finds both - `search(S, 0, 4)` is (0, 4) and `search(S, 0, 3)` is (0, 2) - and both verbs made `(*PRUNE)`, or deleted, give upstream both. New entry `overlapped-skip-missing-match-reversed`, keyed on the walk alone; the three reversed entries are now disjoint by count (equal, upstream longer, upstream shorter). **This is the second time a seed the gate does not use has found a real family** (S33 was the first), and it cost about forty seconds.
- 2026-09-13 **A test that passes with its `(*SKIP)` deleted is not automatically a weak test.** S40d's third blind pass raised exactly that about the new pinned test, and it is the point rather than a weakness: this port's correct answer IS the verb-free answer, because a moved slice no longer carries into the next match. What says the test pins something is the mutation it fails under - delete `state.SliceEnd = state.InitialSliceEnd;` from `Matcher`'s per-match reset and it goes red - not whether the verb changes the expected value. Recorded in the test's own comment so it is not re-raised.
- 2026-09-13 **Upstream's `better` is the improvement loop's TERMINATION as well as its ranking, so a port that ranks differently must split the two tests.** `do_enhanced_fuzzy_match`'s `better = total_errors < fewest_errors` (`_regex.c:17930`) also drives `else break` (`:17972`), and `max_errors` holds every run after the first to fewer errors than the last - so the test is all but always true and exists to stop the loop, not to choose between candidates. S41's first draft replaced the whole of it with this port's cost comparison, which does not re-rank the chain, it CUTS it at the first run that costs more: `fullmatch("(?e)(?:x|xyq){1i+9s+9d<=20}", "yzxyz")` kept a run worse than upstream's answer on the cost this port ranks by AND on the error count upstream ranks by. Found by the blind review, reproduced through `run-oracle.ps1 -Rows` before anything was touched; 54 diverging rows of 2500 against 31 after the fix (`tools/probes/enhancematch-cost-rows.py`, seed 777). **S42 has the same shape to port at `:17647` and must split it the same way.**
- 2026-09-13 **`ENHANCEMATCH`'s dead `same_match` check was measured, and the measurement is stronger than the 2026-09-12 argument for skipping it.** Honouring it saves about 5% of the `BasicMatch` runs (1.42 per enhanced match down to 1.35, three 2000-row `fuzzy` waves) - inside the "at most one extra run" the slice predicted. It is NOT "identical or better" as the slice also predicted: it changes the answer on 18, 15 and 14 rows of 2000 and loses perfect matches, e.g. `(?e)(?:\d+b+?){2i+1d+1s<=2}` over `1bb` goes from an exact match at (0, 2) to a one-substitution match at (0, 3). The override's answer is upstream's on every changed row, because the wave is green with it at three seeds. A lower bound: the experiment guarded the check with "a previous best exists", which upstream's dead code does not.
- 2026-09-13 **`best_fuzzy_counts` stays unported in `SaveBestMatch`/`RestoreBestMatch` even though S41 ported the helpers, because upstream has no `best_fuzzy_changes` beside it.** POSIX saves a match and then fails on purpose to look for a longer one; the backtracking unrecords the fuzzy changes, so restoring the counts alone leaves a count of 1 over an empty changes list and `match_fuzzy_changes` (`:20504`) reads past the end - `regex.match(r'(?p)(?:cat){e<=1}', 'caz').fuzzy_changes` segfaults on 2026.7.19 where `.fuzzy_counts` answers `(1, 0, 0)`. This narrows ledger entry 9, whose "`match` is not affected, only `search`" was about the matching call and not about reading the changes. In a memory-safe language the same asymmetry is a match reporting one substitution and no substitution position, so S42 takes the counts and the changes together or neither.
- 2026-09-13 **An `ExpectedDivergences` entry that covers only part of its own family is better than one widened to cover all of it.** `enhancematch-ranks-by-cost` classifies a cost-ranking divergence only where the two answers are otherwise the same match. A cheaper match can also sit at a different span, and then the `sub`, `split` and `finditer` counts differ too - 31 such rows against 12 classified on the 2500-row probe wave, none of them dearer than upstream. Widening the predicate to take them would also swallow "this port answered a different span", which is what a real engine defect looks like and is the one thing the list must never hide. The rows stay reported, and the entry says so.
- 2026-09-13 **`BESTMATCH` ships with upstream's error-count ranking, because the owner's cost rule cannot be reached from `do_best_fuzzy_match`'s second pass.** Issue 470's own example is the proof: `(?b)(voices){1i+1d+2s<=2}` over `voixes voicees` should answer the cost-1 insertion `voicees` and never can, because the FIRST pass holds the next run to fewer ERRORS than the one it has (`_regex.c:17675`), both candidates are one error, and the search stops at `voixes`. Reaching it needs a cost BOUND inside `basic_match` - `state->max_cost = state->total_cost - 1`, which releases up to 2015.09.28 had and the 2015.11.5 issue-165 **hang fix** replaced with `max_errors` throughout - so it is a change to the shared fuzzy predicates on every mode's hot path, with a hang risk named in its own changelog, and not a line in `DoBestFuzzyMatch`. Layering cost into the equal-count tie-break instead was measured and rejected: issue 470 unchanged, and the `fuzzy` wave goes from 0 to **3, 2 and 3** divergences of 2000 at seeds 7, 4242 and 20260913, every one a cheaper match at a DIFFERENT SPAN - in an overlapped scan a LOST match, 4 where upstream has 5 - which is precisely the family the entry above refuses to classify. The whole change or none of it. `Gaps.Engine.FuzzyBestMatchTests.Bestmatch_still_agrees_with_upstream_on_the_issue_470_example` is what turns red when it lands.
- 2026-09-13 **Upstream's four `*_best_list` functions and three `*_best_changes_list` functions are not ported, because `List<T>` already is them.** A collection expression is `init`, going out of scope is `fini`, `Clear` is `clear` and `Add` is `add_to`; only `RE_BestEntry` and `add_best_fuzzy_changes` have any content, as `Engine.BestEntry` and `Matcher.AddBestFuzzyChanges`. The S42 slice file asked for all four kept as named methods "so PORTMAP's row 428 maps"; four one-line wrappers buy nothing a PORTMAP sentence does not, and `init_fuzzy_changes_list` set that convention in row 417 already.
- 2026-09-13 **A generator's two round-robins pair fully only if their lengths are coprime, and the `fuzzy` generator's are coprime by luck.** The operation is `ALL_OPERATIONS[i % 8]` and the subject alphabet is `FUZZY_SUBJECT_ALPHABETS[i % 3]`. Adding a fourth alphabet band makes `gcd(4, 8) = 4` and LOCKS each operation to exactly one alphabet: `search`, `match`, `subf` and `finditer` then draw no astral subject at all, at any row count (measured seed 7, 2000 rows, 0 of 250 each). Found by S42's blind review. The fix is to index by `i // len(ALL_OPERATIONS)`, which makes the pairing exhaustive rather than accidental; it is written into the comment and NOT applied, because it reshuffles every fuzzy row and doing so uncovered a real `ENHANCEMATCH` defect that S42 was not the slice to fix.
- 2026-09-13 **The checkpoint rule's first live firing crashed the driver**, because `Write-SliceLogEntry`'s `ValidateSet` did not list the new outcome: "The argument "checkpoint" does not belong to the set completed,failed,parked,rate-limited" (`.scratch/driver-s42.err`). The commit survived - the crash came after the verdict and before any rollback - so the outcome was the intended one by accident. Fixed with a Pester test on the writer; S42's first sitting is written into `slice-log.jsonl` by hand from its session log (80,631,260 tokens, the driver's own four-field sum), so the phase close's cost figures are complete.
- 2026-09-13 **`BESTMATCH` ranks by cost through a SECOND WALK over the slice, not through a tie-break, because the owner's rule is lexicographic and one scalar budget cannot express a lexicographic order.** A budget that holds the next run to a strictly lower COST cannot let an equal-cost run through to be judged on its error count; one that holds it to strictly fewer ERRORS prunes the cheaper-but-equal-count match that is all of issue 470. So `Matcher.DoBestFuzzyMatch` walks twice: walk 0 bounded by `MatchState.MaxCost` answers "what is the cheapest match in this slice?", then upstream's own walk (`:17612-17676`) runs unchanged with that cost pinned, which makes its fewest-errors-then-earliest rule the owner's tie-break and leaves the second pass untouched. `MaxCost` and the conjuncts it adds to `any_error_permitted` and `this_error_permitted` are release 2015.09.28's own lines (`:9923`, `:9936`, `:16352`), removed by the 2015.11.5 issue-165 hang fix. S42's first sitting recorded that layering cost into the second pass's tie-break "buys nothing"; that was right, and the reason is this, which it did not have.
- 2026-09-13 **The cost walk is taken only for a pattern with ONE fuzzy section and a WEIGHTED cost equation, and both conditions are load-bearing rather than defensive.** Nesting: an error made inside an inner section is priced at the inner rates while it is being made - which is what the predicates check the bound against - and at the outer rates once `END_FUZZY` merges the counts, which is what `state->total_cost` then reports. The two numbers differ, the budget never bites, and because `start_pos` does not advance the walk HANGS: `(?b)(?:(?:b\W){e:0}){1i+2d+1s<=4}` over `'B-\U0001D518'` re-found the same cost-4, two-error match under a cost-3 budget until it was killed. Weighting: where the three kinds cost the same, a match's cost is a fixed multiple of its error count, so the walk provably cannot change the answer - and it is not free, at 1964 runs of `BasicMatch` against upstream's 1600 over the 665 `(?b)` rows of the committed seed-7 fuzzy wave, +22.8% for nothing. `PatternObject.HasWeightedFuzzyCosts` is set in `NodeCompiler.BuildFuzzy` from the bytecode's three cost words. **A term the equation omits costs NOTHING, not one**, so `2d+1s<4` is weighted - which is why `test_fuzzy#44` diverges and why a `1d+1s` control would have been no control at all.
- 2026-09-13 **One ported test asserts this port's answer rather than upstream's: `test_fuzzy#44`, and S42's first sitting was wrong to record that none would.** `(?b)(foobar){i<=1,d<=2,s<=3,2d+1s<4}` over `FuzzyTestData.Scattered` answers (34, 39) upstream and (26, 33) here. The first sitting's brute force ranked the candidates `finditer` returns, and those are already error-minimised per position, so the cost-2 match at (26, 33) was never in the set it ranked. **Upstream's own engine is the proof it is real**: tighten the equation to `2d+1s<3` and `regex.search` finds exactly (26, 33) with two substitutions and one free insertion; tighten to `<2` and nothing matches, so cost 2 is the floor. Measured on regex 2026.7.19. Recorded per the never-weaken-a-test rule; the evidence is in the comment on the test.
- 2026-09-13 **The `fuzzy` generator no longer pairs a weighted cost equation with `(?e)` or `(?b)`, because a differential oracle cannot judge a comparison the two engines are DEFINED to answer differently.** Adding `(?b)` to the generator turned the wave red at 4 rows of 2000 at each of three seeds, all twelve weighted-under-a-ranking-flag and all twelve this port answering more cheaply. Classifying them instead cannot be made strict: `sub`, `subf` and `split` rows record a string and no per-match counts, so for eight of the twelve there is no cost to compare and the only available predicate is "the spans differ" - which is also exactly what a real engine defect looks like. What replaces the coverage is `tools/probes/enhancematch-cost-rows.py`, extended to draw both flags and read for the INVARIANT (this port's answer is never DEARER) rather than for a green light: 252 divergences of 2500 at seed 777, 128 cheaper, **0 dearer**, 3 equal-cost-but-earlier, 9 no match at all, 112 unscorable. Turning the cost walk off leaves 19, every one an `(?e)` row.
- 2026-09-13 **Upstream `BESTMATCH` loses a match that plain fuzzy matching finds when the best fit needs two trailing insertions, and this port reproduces it - ledger entry 12.** `regex.fullmatch(r'(?b)(?:x){e<=3}', 'xyz')` is `None` where the same pattern without `(?b)` answers `(0, 2, 0)`; one trailing insertion is fine and two are not. The guard at `:15515-15517` double-counts - for a single fuzzy section `state->fuzzy_counts` already holds the merged inner counts, so *n* trailing insertions need `max_errors` above *2n-1* - and the second pass climbs only to `fewest_errors`, so it cannot re-find the match the first pass found. Fixing it is a Phase 6 inherited-bug slice, not S42's. The cost walk does not cause it and widens its reach: 9 rows of the 2500-row probe match nothing with the walk on and none with it off, all of them `fullmatch` with insertion-heavy fits. That is the stated price of the ranking change until the bug is fixed.
- 2026-09-13 **A cost budget needs TWO things upstream's error budget gets for free, and both were found by blind review rather than by reasoning.** First, the three constraint predicates bound the section CURRENTLY OPEN, not the whole match, exactly as they do for the error count - upstream's whole-match backstop is `if (state->total_errors > state->max_errors) goto backtrack` at `END_FUZZY` (`:12486`) and it has no cost twin, because upstream has no running cost. Without that twin a pattern entering one section more than once (a group call, a recursion, a repeat) spends the budget twice and `DoBestFuzzyMatch`'s cost walk HANGS: `(?b)((?:a){1i+2d+1s<=1})(?1)` over `bb`. Second, `MatchState.TotalCost` and `TotalErrors` are SNAPSHOTS written at `END_FUZZY`, and a match can succeed on a path whose last `END_FUZZY` belongs to a branch that was backtracked out of - so both can be stale where `MatchState.FuzzyCounts`, the counts the caller is given as `Match.FuzzyCounts`, is live. Ranking on a stale number scores a genuinely cheaper run as equal and hangs the same way: `(?b)((?:abc){e<=2,2i+1d+3s<=4}(?1)?)` over `bb` reported cost 4 for a match whose live counts cost 2. **The rule that came out of it: whenever this port is the one ranking, it reads the live counts**, priced by `PatternObject.SingleFuzzyNode`, which `rankByCost`'s one-section condition guarantees is the only section. Walk 1 is upstream's walk and keeps upstream's fields, stale exactly where upstream's are.
- 2026-09-13 **An upstream row the recorder cannot get an answer for is now recorded as `resource` and skipped, instead of aborting the whole run.** `ENVIRONMENT_FAILURES` (MemoryError, RecursionError, OverflowError) used to `raise SystemExit`, so one unanswerable row threw away every other row of a six-seed 126,000-row wave. That reasoning was only ever about not RECORDING such a row as an `error` outcome - which would score a port that answers as diverging and a port that also blows up as agreeing - and never about aborting being necessary. `resource` is the exact shape S40a gave `timeout` and is treated identically by `OracleComparer`: not asked of this port, counted in the summary line, never a divergence. The composed fuzzy wave made these routine rather than rare - a repeat whose body can match empty, beside a fuzzy section, gives upstream nothing to progress on and it allocates until MemoryError in a second or two - and three seeds of the S43 wave carry 4 of them. **A first draft blamed `(?b)`, and the blind review killed that with a measurement**: over `'bb.a\r.'`, `(?b)(?P<g1>\p{L}*)+?(?:ab){e<=1}` raises MemoryError in 1.78s, `(?e)` in its place in 1.76s and NO ranking flag at all in 1.75s, while `(?P<g1>\p{L}+)+?(?:ab){e<=1}` - the same shape with a body that must consume - answers in 0.00s. It is the no-progress mechanism of ledger 14, not a BESTMATCH defect.
- 2026-09-13 **`SaveBestMatch`/`RestoreBestMatch` now copy the fuzzy counts AND the change list, where upstream copies only the counts - ledger entry 9's port side, closed.** S41 and S42 both deliberately left `best_fuzzy_counts` unported on the rule "both or neither", because upstream has no `best_fuzzy_changes` beside it and taking the counts alone imports the contradiction that makes upstream's C read freed storage. S43 took the pair. It was not theoretical: before the fix `(?p)(?:abc){e<=1}` over `'axc'` reported `(0, 3)` with counts `(0, 0, 0)` here - the substitution erased by the POSIX restore - where upstream reports `(1, 0, 0)` for the same span. **No oracle wave could ever have caught it**, because upstream faults rendering the changes of exactly the rows that would show it; it was found by hand writing the pinned tests. Design spec amendment 20 is the authority for fixing an inherited bug here rather than carrying it.
- 2026-09-13 **The crash condition in ledger entry 9 is a fuzzy match that SPENT an error, not "a fuzzy section".** Every POSIX row whose `fuzzy_counts` are `(0, 0, 0)` is safe and every row with a non-zero count faults, any error kind, with or without an alternation, inline or by flag - fourteen rows, `tools/probes/upstream-posix-fuzzy-changes-crash.py`. The earlier wording would have had a maintainer try an exact-matching POSIX fuzzy pattern and fail to reproduce it. A first draft of the probe thought an alternation with a shorter branch first was necessary, because every negative control it happened to try was also a zero-error match - which is the reason the probe now carries both halves of the condition as rows.
- 2026-09-13 **`interactions` suppresses POSIX on any row carrying a fuzzy section, and does not draw a self-recursive call round one.** Both are upstream defects rather than generator taste, and both kill the recorder: the first faults the interpreter with no exception to catch (ledger 9), the second raises MemoryError (ledger 14). A narrower rule than "no POSIX on a fuzzy row" was rejected because the faulting condition is a spent error, which depends on the SUBJECT - nothing readable off the pattern is a safe test. For the recursion, a guard that forces progress was tried and measured insufficient: it is safe for every constraint in ledger 14's table, and a 600-row wave still raised MemoryError on four real drawn rows, because progress bounds the depth and not the branching.
- 2026-09-13 **S36's deferred question - whether `interactions` should be recorded prefilter-free - is answered NO, with a measurement.** S36 left it to "the slice that judges those rows". All seven divergences of S43's first composed three-seed 6000-row wave were re-recorded with `interactions` added to `PREFILTER_FREE_GENERATORS`, and not one of them changes: same answer, same span, same count. So the family is not `locate_required_string`, the change would buy nothing, and it would alter how every `interactions` row is recorded. `.scratch/prefilter-test.py` in the S43 session; the seven rows and their verdicts are in STATE.md.
- 2026-09-13 **The empty-section rule behind ledger 14 is a PREDICTOR, not a proof, and the generator's exclusion rests on the measured table instead.** "A fuzzy section that a budget can empty by deletions blows up a self-recursive call" accounts for nine of the eleven constraints `INTERACTION_FUZZY_CONSTRAINTS` draws - `{e<=2}`, `{1<=e<=2}` and `{2i+1d+1s<=2}` raise MemoryError at two atoms and none of the eleven does at three. It fails on `{e<=2,i<=1}` and `{e<=2,s<=1}`, which cap the total at two, cap no deletions, and are safe in 0.00s where the bare `{e<=2}` is not. No mechanism was measured for the difference, so the claim is stated with its exceptions rather than as a law. Found by S43's blind review, which also caught the table omitting `{2i+1d+1s<=2}` - a constraint the generator can draw and that does blow up.
- 2026-09-13 **A seed the phase has not used is worth more than more rows at a seed it has, and S43's close is the fifth slice in a row to prove it.** The Phase 5 exit gate asks for the default wave at three seeds AND `fuzzy`/`interactions` at a fourth. Classifying the seven known rows turned the first three green; the fourth immediately drew three more unjudged divergences from a generator pair already swept at 6000 rows three times - `partial-retry-carried-slice-forward` (new), a fourth `partial-retry-reversed-slice` row, and the row that gave ledger entry 8 a three-item reproduction. VERIFICATION rule 7a says this about a wave; it is now recorded about a PHASE, and Phase 6's hardening slices should be scoped on seeds rather than on row counts. A new seed costs about a minute.
- 2026-09-13 **Ledger entry 8's "no minimal form exists" claim was wrong, and it was wrong because every earlier minimisation shrank along the wrong axis.** The entry told a report not to claim a minimal form: shrinking its rows produced patterns on which this port answers what upstream answers. All of those rows hold the call inside a *conditional* inside a *repeat*, and the cuts removed the repeat or the conditional - the pieces the surrounding match needed - rather than the call's own setting. Seed 99991's row 10201 has the call in a bare lookbehind, and cutting that one reaches `(?P<g1>\w)(?<=(?&g1))\W` over `'aa '` without ever losing the divergence, on 2026.7.19 and on 2026.9.10. **The subject must be three characters, not two**: at two, this port also answers None, and not because it shares the bug - the unrelated `min_width` inflation S40c pinned refuses a two-character subject before matching starts, masking this defect at exactly one length. The pinned test was first written with `'a '` and FAILED, which is how the mask was found rather than assumed.
- 2026-09-13 **The forward and reversed halves of the carried-slice defect get separate `ExpectedDivergences` entries, following `overlapped-skip-stale-slice` and its `-reversed` twin rather than one widened entry.** A `(*SKIP)` moves `slice_start` forwards and `slice_end` under `(?r)`, and what the unrestored bound costs differs accordingly: reversed it hides an anchor, so upstream answers a degenerate zero-width partial; forwards the two engines agree on the SPAN and differ in which alternative the partial pass could still enter, so the difference surfaces as the error spent and the group captured. One entry spanning both would have to state each half separately anyway. Both are judged by the `(*PRUNE)` control - same backtracking pruning, no bound moved - which on the forward row is the whole of the evidence rather than a corroboration, because upstream's anchored door is not a second opinion there.
- 2026-09-13 **`tools/check-symbols.py` pins the 38 names PORTMAP accounts for in prose, and fails if that set changes in EITHER direction.** The first build exited non-zero on those 38 and would have done so on every run for ever, which is a gate nobody can act on. They are covered by family rows that name a few members and take the rest in prose - "every `*_dealloc` and `*_deepcopy`", "the `scanner_*`/`splitter_*` iterator protocol", "`re_alloc` ... `release_state_lock`" - so they are accounted for by a human and invisible to a script by construction. Pinning them makes a NEW unaccounted function fail, and a name that PORTMAP later lists individually fail too, so the pin cannot rot. Same contract as `tests/parity-baseline.json` and `ExpectedDivergences.cs`, chosen for the same reason.
- 2026-09-13 **The four `Seam.For` calls left in `src/` are unreachable `default` arms, not unported capability, and are deliberately left as they are.** S43's slice file asks for zero seams. All four sit at the end of exhaustive switches (`Matcher.cs:944`, `:2046`, `:7581`, `:8852`) and each carries a comment naming the opcodes that reach the switch and are handled above; with Phase 5's fuzzy opcodes delivered, nothing can reach them but a corrupt opcode. Changing them to a different exception would say something truer in a stack trace and would also change behaviour at a phase close for no functional gain, so the count is reported as 4 with this reason rather than driven to 0. A Phase 6 slice touching the dispatch loop should fold them into whatever it does about `RE_ERROR_ILLEGAL`.
- 2026-09-13 **`DoBestFuzzyMatch`'s first walk keeps `MaxErrors = fewestErrors - 1`, upstream's `:17675`, and a session that changes it hangs the suite.** S43's second sitting left that line as `fewestErrors` uncommitted, with no rationale anywhere in its notes; the suite then spun for ever (the walk re-finds the same equal-error match at the same `start_pos`), the driver's landing check hung on the test host, and the orchestrator had to stop the driver by hand. Two process consequences, both for Phase 6's first maintenance pass: `check-ratchet.ps1` runs the suite with no timeout, so a hang in the tree becomes a hung driver rather than a red ratchet - it needs a bound; and a hang is the worst possible test failure, so the suite wants a per-test timeout (TUnit's `[Timeout]`) at the assembly level, so that an engine loop shows up as one named red test.
- 2026-09-13 **Phase 6 authored as S44-S57** by the orchestrator (Fable session, no subagents) at the owner's request, from S43's handover, the ROADMAP's Phase 6 section, the ledger and amendments 12-17 and 21. Grouped the inherited-bug fixes by mechanism (four slices) rather than one slice per ledger entry; split the issue sweep into triage-and-reproduce then fix so the failing tests exist before the fixing session starts; split mutation testing into tooling-and-calibration then survivors, with the engine's Stryker runs done detached overnight by the orchestrator in calibrated chunks (owner: many small runs, long wall time acceptable); added S51 timeouts and cancellation (amendment 22). Three slices need orchestrator action before launch because the driver cannot install tools or reach the tracker; each says so in a "Before launch" section.
- 2026-09-13 **Slice sessions now know their deadline and can be messaged; a failed sitting keeps its green commit; the suite and the ratchet are bounded.** Six changes, owner-approved: (1) `tools/session-hook.ps1`, a PreToolUse hook in `.claude/settings.json`, injects `[driver deadline] N minutes` from 30 minutes out and relays a one-shot `.scratch/orchestrator-message.txt`; silent unless `FUZZY_SLICE_SESSION=1`, which only the driver sets. Proven with a throwaway Haiku `claude -p` session that quoted the planted token and "11 minutes deadline". (2) The driver sets that variable and writes `.scratch/session-deadline.txt` per sitting. (3) `Undo-FailedSlice -KeepHeadIfGreen` re-runs the ratchet on the clean HEAD after the stash and resets to HEAD rather than to session start when it is green, so a checkpoint commit survives a later kill or a dirty ending. (4) `tools/heartbeat.sh` reports `left=<n>m` from the deadline file and alarms at 20 remaining, tracking the sitting rather than the driver run. (5) `[assembly: Timeout(120_000)]` on the test project and `--timeout 20m` on the ratchet's `dotnet test`, so an engine loop is a named red test and never a hung driver. (6) `budget.json`: slice caps 30 a day and 60 a week (owner: 33% of the weekly allowance unused, renewing 14:00 UK 2026-09-14), sitting cap 240 minutes now that the kill is not the only backstop.
- 2026-09-13 **The upstream pin is 2026.9.10, and 2026.7.19 is the release that stands in for the old pin in every before/after measurement.** The old submodule pin, tag `2026.8.12`, was never published to PyPI - `pip install regex==2026.8.12` fails, and the newest release before the four issue fixes is 2026.7.19. `git -C upstream diff 2026.7.19..2026.8.12 -- src/ regex/` is one line, `__version__`, so 2026.7.19 IS the old pin's behaviour and every "before" figure in S44 is taken against it. This also settles retrospectively that the oracle interpreter running 2026.7.19 against a 2026.8.12 submodule was not a real disagreement, only a labelling one. Tag `2026.9.10` is also upstream's head (`git -C upstream diff 2026.9.10..origin/hg` is empty), so nothing unreleased had to be taken ahead of the release.
- 2026-09-13 **Byte-identity against PyPI is proven against the WHEEL raw and the SDIST after line-ending normalisation, and the difference is worth stating.** Amendment 7 asks that `upstream/regex/` match the wheel and `upstream/src/` the sdist. The wheel's four `.py` files match our checkout byte for byte with no normalisation, and the sdist's four `src/` files and four `regex/*.py` files match only after CRLF/LF folding: the sdist ships LF, the wheel ships CRLF, and this repo's `* text=auto eol=lf` still checks out CRLF on Windows. So the raw wheel match is a coincidence of two CRLF sources, not a stronger result, and a future sync that reports "raw identical" for both has measured something wrong. `.scratch/byte_identity.py` in the S44 session; the numbers are in the PORTMAP sync-log row.

"Ported faithfully" and "changed nothing" are different claims and both are now on record, so a future sync that sees an answer move on this shape reads it as news rather than as this fix arriving late.

The clamp that stops the runaway retreat also stops the one legitimate retreat step the match needs, once a `(*SKIP)` has moved `slice_start` above the repeat. **The decisive evidence is upstream contradicting itself**: the same pattern over `'aaxb'`, where the match completes, answers `(0, 4)` and takes the identical retreat. This port carries both clamps and keeps the match because S40b restores the slice bounds before the partial pass, so the port is not diverging by omitting the fix. It went unseen because sessions select subsets: S43's own command is `--slices S37,...,S43`, which steps over the broken ids by accident rather than by design. S44 ran the suite in two passes, the second with an explicit `--ids` list of the 39 survivors from S31 on. Two things are owed and neither is this slice's scope: re-anchor the five (each needs its own judgement about what the control should now mutate), and make the runner report an unresolvable control and carry on rather than abort, so a broken anchor costs one control instead of the rest of the suite.
- 2026-09-13 **The sync invalidated PORTMAP's `_regex.c` line ranges, `tools/check-symbols.py` caught three of them, and the rest are measured and FLAGGED rather than silently rewritten.** PRs 615-618 added ~65 lines in the middle of `_regex.c`, so every cited line number after about `:18245` now points at different code. Not rewritten here, for three reasons: it is outside a slice scoped to four changelog entries; a several-hundred-line mechanical diff is one a blind review cannot usefully check; and it needs an owner decision first, because historical documents (`docs/plan/slices/done/`, the dated decision notes) record what was true at the time and should NOT be rewritten, while PORTMAP, `src/` and `tests/` comments are live and should. Proposed as a maintenance item with a tool: derive the old-to-new line map from the diff, rewrite only the live set, and verify every rewritten reference lands on identical text. **Until that lands, a line number in a comment is accurate as of the slice that wrote it, not as of the current pin.**
- 2026-09-13 **`tools/run-controls.py` mutates tracked source in place, so a slice must not read or commit its working tree while a control run is in flight.** S44 ran the control suite in the background and, half an hour later, found `stack.PushSize(repeatData.Count)` reading `repeatData.Count + 1` in `git diff` - S28-C's mutation, caught mid-run, in a diff being read to review the slice's own changes. Nothing was committed, but the two failure modes are obvious once seen: a `git commit -a` during a run commits somebody's deliberate bug, and a diff read during one is not the slice's diff. The runner restores each file after each control, so the window is seconds per control and hours per suite. Two rules from here: **finish the controls before reviewing the diff or committing**, and treat a crashed control run as leaving a mutated tree that needs `git checkout` before anything else. Worth a guard in the runner - a lock file the slice skill tells sessions to check - which is a maintenance item rather than this slice's.
- 2026-09-13 **The `fewestErrors` hang was never a hand edit: it is control S42-1B, left applied because a KILL skips the runner's restore. Both sittings were killed inside that control's window, which is eight minutes wide because the mutant does not diverge - it HANGS, `TIMEOUT the consumer did not finish within 240s` at both seeds. So the rule "treat a crashed control run as leaving a mutated tree" is not a precaution, it is the diagnosis for two lost sittings, and the first thing a recovering session should do is `git diff src/` against `tools/controls.json`. Nobody edited that line by hand, and the entry above should be read with that correction.

S42-1B's two timeouts left `FuzzyRegex.OracleTests` PIDs 34428 and 26696 spinning on the mutated engine and holding `tests/FuzzyRegex.OracleTests/bin/Debug/net10.0/FuzzyRegex.dll`, so the next seven controls all reported `NO REPORT` on `MSB3027 ... Exceeded retry count of 10` - twice, in two separate runs. The runner reports that identically to a control that ran and found nothing, which is the dangerous part: a suite can go quietly dark after one hang. S44 could not clear it, because killing a process is outside the slice session's permissions, so the seven are recorded as NOT MEASURED with the re-run command rather than assumed fine. The runner needs to kill the process tree on timeout, and to distinguish "did not run" from "ran and found nothing".

S45-A takes `case-folding` at 2000 rows from 4/11/8 expected (seeds 7, 4242, 31337) to 0/4/3; S45-B takes it to 4/7/6. Two limits remain: the text scan misses a pattern reaching the codepoint by `ı`, `\N{...}`, `\L<name>` or an unnamed range (a false negative, which reddens a run and gets judged - the safe direction); and a fabricated failure on `(?i)ı.` over `ıx` is still classified (a false positive). - 2026-09-14 **Ledger entry 12's mechanism was right and its statement of the SYMPTOM was wrong, and the difference matters to the report.** The entry said *n* trailing insertions need `max_errors` above *2n-1*, which reads as though a budget buys the match back. Under `(?b)` the caller never sets `max_errors`: `do_best_fuzzy_match`'s second pass sets it to `fewest_errors` = *n*, so the doubled guard needs *n > 2n-2* and the match is lost for every *n >= 2* **at every budget**.
