- 2026-09-15 (S52, sitting 10): **upstream holds TWO contradictory rules for a reversed partial that runs out of text, this port holds both as well, and gate row 104366 is therefore NOT pinned - the owner is asked to rule.** `init_match` sets `text_end` to the SLICE end but `text_start` to 0 (`upstream/src/_regex.c:18442-18446`), five lines under its own comment at `:18435-18437` that "the end of the slice behaves like the end of the string"; every node handler then asks `text_pos <= text_start` (`:6747` and about thirty more) and reports nothing at a non-zero slice start, while `search_start` asks `start_pos < slice_start` and reports a partial there (`:8400-8405`). So the answer depends on whether the optimiser picked the pattern's leading string as the search test - a decision about speed. Measured over 33 cells in 7 blocks: 23 agree and 10 differ, and the 10 split BOTH ways - 3 where this port reports a partial and upstream does not (`(?r)ya(.*)\b` and `(?r)ya(.?)\b` over a one-character slice, differing from the lazy spelling only in preference order, which cannot decide whether a match exists) and 7 where upstream does and this port does not (the drawn row and the `(?r)ab(.*?)\b` empty-slice family). Each engine reports a reversed partial at a non-zero slice start in some shapes and refuses it in others, the shapes do not line up, and neither set contains the other - so "upstream contradicts itself" does not put this port in the right here. The probable reading is that `text_start = 0` exists for the `^`/`\A` open-start rule and the partial handlers reuse it for a different question whose bound is `slice_start` - which would make this an INHERITED BUG to fix rather than a divergence to pin, an engine change wherever that question is asked - 38 sites in upstream, 9 in this port's `Engine/Matcher.cs`. Nothing changed; both probes committed (`tools/probes/upstream-reversed-partial-ignores-the-slice-start.py` and its port half).

- 2026-09-15 **Ledger 24 ruled: a reversed partial runs out of text at the slice start (owner, Option B of `docs/plan/upstream-reports/ledger-24-briefing.md`); S52d authored.** Grounds, in order of weight: a partial means the pattern needs characters and the matchable text has run out, and a reversed match cannot consume text before `pos` (measured on 2026.9.10); upstream's own comment, optimiser and reversed string helpers already say the slice edge is the edge, and its changelog repaired partial reporting seven times without ever treating a lost partial as intended; the greedy/lazy and minimum-width invariants hold only this way; symmetry with the forward `endpos`; Boost.Regex, .NET's substring overload and PCRE2 all treat a caller-imposed bound as the end of the text. `^`, `\A`, `` and lookbehind are untouched. Outcome (c), fixed here, not filed. S52d runs after S52c so the invariant checker proves it over the wave.

- 2026-09-15 (S52, sitting 14): **a recorded `timeout` is comparable ONLY where the row carries its own budget, and the licence is a measured margin rather than the deadline's existence.** The blanket ten seconds S40a added is a fact about the recording machine - a row needing eleven and a row needing nine record differently on two runs - which is why the consumer skips those and must go on skipping them. A row from the new `timeout` generator carries a `timeout` field instead, drawn from ten shapes measured still running after TWENTY TIMES that budget on both engines (80 of 80 shape-by-operation cells each, 160 of 160 across the two), and on those rows "the call raised rather than running past its budget" is a property of the engine. `OracleRow.Timeout` is the whole discriminator: present, the row is compared and a `RegexMatchTimeoutException` raised while matching is the agreement; absent, the old skip stands. A port that ANSWERS one is a divergence on purpose - Phase 7 optimisation reddening a row here is the signal to redraw the family, not to soften the rule.
- 2026-09-15 (S52, sitting 14): **upstream is not vulnerable to the textbook catastrophic patterns, and a timeout generator built from them would have tested nothing.** Measured on regex 2026.9.10: `(a+)+$`, `(a*)*$`, `(.*,)*z`, `(a+)+\1$` and `(?=(a+)+$)a` all answer in milliseconds at every length tried, because a nested repeat over a single-character body collapses. What blows up is an AMBIGUOUS ALTERNATION under a repeat, and the ambiguity must survive compilation - `(?:ab|a)+$` and `(?:[ab]|a)+$` are fast, and so are `(?i)(a|A)+$` and the reversed `(?r)(a|a)+^`, so the generator draws no flags at all. The nine rejected shapes live in `tools/probes/timeout-row-margin.py` as runnable data rather than as prose, and the probe exits 1 if one of them ever becomes catastrophic.

- 2026-09-16 (S52b): **`Match.FuzzyChanges` was not safe to read from two threads, and the fix is a single reference.** The cache was a `FuzzyChanges?` - a `HasValue` flag plus three list references - published with `??=`. A four-field write is not atomic, so a reader could see the flag set and a list still null; reproduced on 64 threads, three runs of three. Now a `StrongBox<FuzzyChanges>`: one atomic reference publication of an idempotent value. `Lazy<T>` was rejected because it allocates on every match whether or not the property is read, and eager computation because `SplitFuzzyChanges` allocates three lists unconditionally. **The deterministic guard is the rule, not the stress test:** any field a read writes must be reference-typed, which fails instantly on a value-typed cache where the race probe is sampling.
- 2026-09-16 (S52b): **the pattern graph's forty mutable fields are allowlisted, and the allowlist is proved by a snapshot rather than by argument.** All forty are written inside `Engine.PatternObject.Compile` and the passes it calls, invoked from one place - the `FuzzyRegex` constructor - so the graph is finished before the instance exists. Making them readonly would restructure the port away from upstream's shape and cost more at every sync. The two allowlist tests assert subset in both directions, so they pin set equality and a stale entry fails.
- 2026-09-16 (S52b): **a snapshot test must take its baseline BEFORE the first operation, not after a warm-up.** Both blind-review findings were this one mistake: warming up first hid every first-match-only write, which is exactly the shape a Phase 7 lazily computed cache takes and so precisely what the test existed to catch. Fixed by snapshotting before any match. For statics, where a virgin reading cannot be guaranteed in a shared test process, the second defence is running the two workloads over **differently salted subjects**, so a per-subject memo keeps growing instead of saturating after one pass.
- 2026-09-16 (S52b): **a barrier needs real threads, never the thread pool.** The first stress probe used `Parallel.For` with a `Barrier(ProcessorCount * 4)`; `Parallel.For` promises no such concurrency, so `SignalAndWait` waited on participants injected at about one per half-second. It was a latent hang AND a feeble test - it found the tear once in ~57s and not at all in a 15s budget. On 64 dedicated `Thread`s, 20,000 rounds run in ~2s and caught the same defect 3 of 3.
- 2026-09-16 (S52b): **a test seam must not be a settable static.** The slice asked for a debug `ArrayPool` wrapper the whole suite runs under; the only seam reaching every `MatchState` is a mutable static on the library, which this slice's own static audit forbids. `ByteStack` took an instance-level `ArrayPool<byte>?` constructor parameter instead, and since every `ArrayPool` call in `src/FuzzyRegex` is the one `Rent` and two `Return`s inside that class, testing it exhaustively covers the same code. Scope narrowed deliberately and recorded, not quietly dropped.
- 2026-09-16 (S52b): **never instruct a verifier or reviewer to `git checkout -- <file>` while the slice's work is uncommitted.** Mine did, after each control, and for two controls the file was one the slice had modified but not committed - so the checkout discarded the slice's own work. It was reconstructed and I checked both diffs line by line before accepting them. Commit a checkpoint before the verifier runs, or brief it to stash and restore.

Walking all 24 entries and asking "which pair of doors contradicted each other here" is what produced the groups, so no claim about upstream's behaviour rests on reasoning about what upstream probably does, and anyone re-reading the ledger can check the list. Sixteen of the 24 are reached; the eight that are not are named at the foot of the file. Highest yield is `posix-chooses-among-flagless-answers` - entries 9, 16 and 23, three ways of breaking one documented contract, for one extra call.

The V0/V1 agreement invariant is rejected outright: its exception list is open-ended, and an invariant whose exceptions are not closed cannot separate a bug from an exception. `reverse-mirrors-forward` is deferred - `(?r)` reverses the scan, not the pattern's semantics, so it needs a transformation whose bugs are indistinguishable from the engine's; `greedy-lazy-existence-agree` reaches the same reversed rows for two extra calls and no transformation, and it is the invariant that handles gate row 104366.

Recording that as "fuzzysearch fails on Windows" would have been a false fact about an external system; OPERATIONS.md records the permission wall and the single command the owner runs to settle it. - 2026-09-16 (S52c, sitting 3): **report ELIGIBILITY beside every invariant's firing count, or a zero means nothing.** "Fired on 0 of 34,721 eligible rows" is cheap evidence of consistency and "fired on 0 of 0" is a check that is not running, and a wave summary prints the two identically - which is the same failure mode the whole slice exists to remove, one level up. `tools/probes/invariant-triage.py` computes eligibility independently of the checker for that reason. - 2026-09-16 (S52c, sitting 3): **gate row 104366 is upstream contradicting itself and this port being self-consistent, which the differential oracle could not have said.** Over a six-cell grid, upstream breaks `greedy-lazy-existence-agree` on two cells - `(?r)\xdfﬁ(.*?)\b` answers a zero-width partial over the empty slice (2, 2) where `(.*)` answers no match - and this port breaks it on none; on every cell where upstream is self-consistent the two engines agree. Greediness orders the candidate set and cannot change its membership. Removing the `(?r)` makes the invariant hold and the engines agree; removing the `\b` does neither, so the mechanism is the reversed partial path. **This does not settle ledger 24's `slice_start`-versus-`text_start` ruling and the row is NOT pinned on it** - that is the owner's call and S52d's slice.
- 2026-09-16 (S52c, sitting 3): **TRE answers 10 of 126,240 wave rows and none of this slice's 10 violation rows, and that is a fact about DIALECTS rather than about TRE.** It offers a search and nothing else, so every anchored operation is out; POSIX ERE has no `\p{...}`, backreference, lookaround, verb or `\K`; and it has no BESTMATCH, no per-section budget and no `fuzzy_changes`. The generators that produce violations are exactly the ones furthest from that core, so on the rows the invariants are for, the second engine is NOT available and the invariants are the instrument. `tools/probes/tre-fuzzy-check.py --self-test` exists because a second engine that is never asked anything and one that agrees print the same thing. **Two of its answers were the probe's own bugs first**: the dialect gate leaked `(?i)`-prefixed patterns, and it defaulted an unnamed error kind to unbounded where upstream documents it as forbidden (`upstream/README.rst:561`). A second engine's disagreement is a hypothesis about the harness before it is one about either engine.
- 2026-09-16 (S52d): **ledger 24 is ruled and fixed: a reversed partial runs out of text at the SLICE START, one rule, asked in one place.** The owner's Option B, applied at fifteen `TextStart` sites in `Engine/Matcher.cs` through `RanOutOnTheLeft(state, textPos)` (and `SteppedPastTheLeft` for the two whose position has already been moved by an error or a scan step, which compare strictly). Three sites that already read `SliceStart` - `IsStringTestPartial`'s two reversed string arms and `BasicMatch`'s reversed scan bound - were routed through the same helper, because the defect being fixed IS two places spelling the same question differently. Reads of `TextStart` that serve `^`, `\A`, `(?m)^`, `(?m)$`, `\b`, `\B`, the grapheme walks and the lookaround slice widening are untouched and listed in the ledger entry, and `tools/probes/upstream-non-zero-pos-guards.py` pins seven of them against upstream.
- 2026-09-16 (S52d): **the ledger's count of nine port sites was six short, and the six were the same question in a different SHAPE.** It had enumerated the `state.TextPos <= state.TextStart && PartialSide == PartialLeft` spelling; `TryMatchAnyRev`, `TryMatchAnyAllRev`, `TryMatchAnyURev` and `TryMatchOneRev` write it as a guard whose else-branch tests `SliceStart`, and `IsTailPartial` writes it as two switch arms. Changing only the nine would have left a reversed one-character TEST answering Rule A while its own opcode arm answered Rule B - the identical defect, moved. **Grep the predicate, not the spelling**: `PartialLeft` found all fifteen where `TextStart` alone found nine and twenty-odd anchors.
- 2026-09-16 (S52d): **S33's `The_narrowed_slice_partial_is_a_per_opcode_answer_and_not_a_general_rule` was the same evidence read the other way, and it is rewritten rather than deleted.** It pinned upstream's `None` on five cells and said in its own comment "pinned so nobody simplifies it into one rule about slice_start"; the ruling is that it IS one rule about the slice start. The five are now asserted as a deliberate divergence with upstream's answers re-measured on 2026.9.10 beside them (`tools/probes/upstream-s33-per-opcode-cells.py`), together with their `(0, 0)` twins, where upstream answers a partial for all four - which is what makes the original five a contradiction rather than a design.
- 2026-09-16 **Unported-members review before Phase 7 (owner request; Fable).** Every row of DIVERGENCES' "Upstream members with no port equivalent" re-examined for API design, correctness and effect on optimisation, against the port's surface, upstream's README and Microsoft Learn (`Regex.EnumerateMatches`/`EnumerateSplits`, `Regex.CacheSize`, `GroupCollection`'s dictionary interface). Seventeen rows stand; none hides a correctness gap. Four decisions change and are packaged as S53b (queued after S53, before S54): lazy enumeration, the three inline-only flags as options (superseding decision D for those three members), the dictionary view on `GroupCollection`, and slicing on `Replace`. The pattern cache is a Phase 7 decision recorded now as PLANNED with `CacheSize`. `docs/plan/OPTIMISATION-NOTES.md` created as the index of deferred optimisations (source comments are the record; a Sonnet verifier checked every cited line, three rows corrected). S53 amended to publish the whole TUnit suite as Native AOT first, which .NET 10 already supports. Full write-up: `docs/plan/2026-09-16-unported-members-review.md`.
- 2026-09-16 (S53): **the Native AOT gate is green and `src/FuzzyRegex` needed no change to make it so** - every one of the five real defects was in the test project, in AwesomeAssertions or in the build. The static half (`IsAotCompatible`, Phase 2) had already done its job: the library produces zero trim and zero AOT warnings, and a published consumer answers all 29 feature-area cases correctly. **`BeEquivalentTo` is the one assertion that cannot run natively** (its equivalency engine reaches `MethodInfo.MakeGenericMethod`): 1,547 of 6,153 tests failed on the first publish, all at that call, and the twelve sites now use `Equivalence`, which renders to sorted lines and compares with `Should().Equal(...)`. A csproj suppression - the slice's other stated option - would not have helped, because these are run-time failures and not warnings. `AotAssertionConventionTests` is the ratchet, and it matches the member name alone so `.BeEquivalentTo<T>(` cannot evade it.
- 2026-09-16 (S53): **the S52b object-graph walk is JIT-only, and is SKIPPED natively rather than weakened.** It reads BCL private fields such as `List<T>._items`, for which native AOT generates no accessor: by default the walk returned ONE slot and all three subset assertions passed over nothing, and with `IlcGenerateCompleteTypeMetadata=true` it threw `NotSupportedException: ... no code was generated for it` and cost 5.3 MB of binary. Rewriting it to enumerate through `IEnumerable` would make it run and would also stop it seeing a list's `_size` and `_version` move, and S52b's rules are permanent. The three rules run under the JIT on all three operating systems in `build-and-ratchet`.
- 2026-09-16 (S53): **a reflection scan that finds nothing makes a subset assertion pass, and three permanent tests had that shape.** The AOT run exposed it, but the defect is not about AOT. Every scan now carries a measured floor (281 library types, 465 static fields, 892 ported test METHODS, 214 test sources, 40 allowlist entries). The ported-test floor was first written at 1,500 from confusing the status board's 1,967 test CASES with test methods and failed immediately - the guard earned its place before it was committed. **A scan-and-report test needs a non-vacuity floor at the same time as the rule.**
- 2026-09-16 (S53): **`PublishAot` belongs on the publish command line, never in the csproj**, for any project whose code asks which runtime it is on. In the csproj the SDK also writes `RuntimeFeature.IsDynamicCodeSupported: false` into the ORDINARY build's runtimeconfig, so the smoke app's own honesty check reported a Native AOT run during a plain `dotnet run` - manufacturing the evidence the gate exists to produce. Found by the blind review. `Assembly.Location` is not the alternative: it trips IL3000.
- 2026-09-16 (S53): **quote the WARM run of a published binary and nothing else.** A freshly written 6.65 MB executable cost 566 ms to reach `Main` on its first execution and 28-30 ms on re-runs of the same file - image load and an antivirus scan of bytes the operating system has not seen. `tools/run-aot-smoke.ps1` now runs the binary twice and labels the second as the one to quote. The verifier added the sharp edge: an incremental re-publish that rewrites byte-identical content does NOT reproduce the penalty, so only genuinely new bytes measure it.
- 2026-09-16 (S53): **a wedged MSBuild node hangs a publish indefinitely, and `-nodeReuse:false -p:UseSharedCompilation=false` is the fix.** Two publishes sat for 600 s each with no `ilc.exe` ever starting; an ordinary `dotnet build` of the same project hung beside them and `dotnet build-server shutdown` hung too; the identical build with those two flags took 31 s. Both AOT scripts pass them. **`tools/check-ratchet.ps1` is NOT fixed and hung the same way** - it is the driver's own success test, so whether it should set `MSBUILDDISABLENODEREUSE` is an owner call rather than a mid-slice edit. Carried in STATE.md.
- 2026-09-16 (S53): **a green ratchet does not mean the repository compiles.** `tools/check-ratchet.ps1` builds only the test project, so the slice reached GREEN with a new sample project that failed CI's own `dotnet build --configuration Release` on two IDE0055 errors. CSharpier writes an interpolation alignment specifier as `{name, -14}` and IDE0055 rejects that space, so a line using one cannot satisfy both gates - pad explicitly instead. **Run `dotnet build` over the solution before committing a slice that adds a project.**
- 2026-09-16 (S53): **a wall-clock millisecond quoted to four digits is a claim that will not reproduce, and this slice made the same mistake three times.** The blind review's second pass caught a probe timing, the verifier caught another 30% out, and the commit-ready re-run of the smoke gate came in at 53.8 ms to `Main` against the 29.7 ms first recorded - all on one machine, in one sitting, while that machine was building. Every timing in S53's notes, ROADMAP and STATE is now a RANGE with its observation count, and the one exact figure - the published binary at 6,972,928 bytes, byte-exact on re-publish and confirmed independently - is flagged as the solid one. **Record a range and the number of runs, or record only what is deterministic.**
- 2026-09-16 (S53b): **decision D is superseded for `ASCII`, `UNICODE` and `WORD` only, and `Options` stops masking them.** They are `FuzzyRegexOptions.Ascii` (0x80), `.Unicode` (0x20) and `.Word` (0x800), upstream's own bits. The visible consequence is that `new FuzzyRegex("a").Options` now reports `Unicode`, because `_main._compile` ORs it into every text pattern (`_main.py:570-574`) - which is upstream's own answer, `regex.compile('(?i)(a)(b)').flags` being `0x2022`, and which the ported `test_getattr` had been recording as a divergence. Ten assertions across five files were updated to upstream's number. `LOCALE` stays out (no .NET meaning), `DEBUG` stays out (prints a parse tree), `TEMPLATE` stays out (upstream implements no behaviour for it).
- 2026-09-16 (S53b): **`GroupCollection` implements `IReadOnlyDictionary<string, Group>`, and the slice file's description of .NET's version was wrong in a way worth recording.** The slice said "`Keys` (named groups only, in number order, as .NET does)". Measured instead (`tools/probes/dotnet-groupcollection-dictionary.ps1`, .NET 10.0.10): the built-in's face is TOTAL - `Count` 4 and `Keys` `[0, 1, a, c]` for `(?<a>a)(b)(?<c>c)?`, an unnamed group keyed by its number as text. So `Keys` is exactly `FuzzyRegex.GroupNames`, and `groupdict` is a filter over it rather than the thing itself. **The lookup is a round trip, not a fallback**: a numeric key resolves only if `GroupNameFromNumber` gives that spelling back, so `"1"` fails on a pattern whose group 1 is named. One `NumberForKey` serves the indexer, `ContainsKey` and `TryGetValue`, because a key `Keys` yields that `ContainsKey` denies is a broken dictionary.
- 2026-09-16 (S53b): **implementing `IReadOnlyDictionary` on a type that is already an `IReadOnlyList` is a SOURCE BREAK, and eleven call sites in this repo proved it.** Two `IEnumerable<T>` faces make `Groups.Select(...)` and `Groups.Skip(...)` fail to infer (CS0411) - exactly as they do on the built-in `GroupCollection`, which took the same break in .NET 5. The fix at a call site is `Groups.Values`, and that is the documented answer in `docs/DIVERGENCES.md`. Doing it before 1.0 is the whole reason the owner queued S53b ahead of S54.
- 2026-09-16 (S53b): **`Replace` and `ReplaceFormat` take `beginning`/`length`, and the rule is that the text OUTSIDE the slice is copied through unchanged.** Measured, not read off the C (`tools/probes/upstream-sub-with-a-slice.py`, regex 2026.9.10): `regex.compile('a').sub('X', 'aaaaa', pos=1, endpos=3)` is `'aXXaa'`. The C agrees and says why - `pattern_subx`'s trailing segment runs to `str_info.length` rather than to the slice end (`:22092`) and `last_pos` starts from `state.text_length`, which is the whole subject's length and not the truncated `text_end`. The engine change is four lines: `Substitution.Subx` takes the pair and clamps it with `ClampIndex` where upstream calls `get_limits`, which is **before** the too-short shortcut and therefore decides it - `'xx'.sub(r'\g<bad', 'xxxxx', pos=4)` returns the subject untouched instead of rejecting the template.
- 2026-09-16 (S53b): **upstream's inverted slice cannot be spelled through `beginning`/`length` at all, and that is the right answer.** `sub('X', 'aaaaa', pos=3, endpos=1)` clamps `endpos` up to `pos` and replaces nothing; here any negative length means "the rest of the subject", so the closest spelling is `(3, 0)` - which gives upstream's answer for the reason a caller would expect rather than by a clamp. Pinned in `Gaps/Substitution/ReplaceSliceTests.cs`.
- 2026-09-16 (S53b): **the lazy walks build one engine state PER STEP, where upstream's scanner holds one for the walk, and the reason is disposal rather than fidelity.** A `MatchState` owns rented buffers; a state held across a `yield return` is one an abandoned `foreach` never returns. So `EnumerateMatches` is a loop over `Iteration.Step` - the same method `Match.NextMatch` uses - and `EnumerateSplits` is `Split`'s loop over that walk. Two consequences are documented rather than hidden: the walk costs one pass over the subject per match (OPTIMISATION-NOTES), and `timeout` bounds each step rather than the whole walk (DIVERGENCES). `Matches` and `Split` stay eager and keep the one-state budget.
- 2026-09-16 (S53b): **`EnumerateSplits` takes no `beginning`/`length`, against the slice file's sketch.** `Split` has none because upstream has none: `pattern_split`'s keyword list is `string, maxsplit, concurrent, timeout` (`_regex.c:22235`). Giving the lazy twin a slice the eager one lacks would break the one property the whole item is for - that the two yield the same pieces - at every non-default slice.
- 2026-09-16 (S53b): **an iterator method must validate its arguments eagerly.** `EnumerateMatches` and `EnumerateSplits` resolve the subject null check, the timeout and the token in a non-iterator method that returns the iterator, so a bad argument is reported from the call rather than from the first `MoveNext`. Pinned by `LazyEnumerationTests.The_arguments_are_checked_when_the_call_is_made_not_when_it_is_walked`.
- 2026-09-16 (S53b): **CA1859 is disapplied for test code only.** It wants a concrete return type wherever an interface would cost a dispatch, which is right in `src/` and backwards in `GroupDictionaryViewTests`, whose whole subject is `GroupCollection`'s interface face - taking the rule's advice there would replace every call under test with the class's own members. Scoped to `tests/**` in `.editorconfig`; `src/` still obeys it.
- 2026-09-16 (maintenance, owner decisions): **the public API is frozen after S53b, enforced by `Microsoft.CodeAnalysis.PublicApiAnalyzers`, and RS0026 is disapplied on the merits.** Phase 7 (optimisation), Phase 8 (docs, Junie-driven) and Phase 9 (demo) run in parallel worktrees while main finishes S54-S57, so any public-surface change must be deliberate and visible: RS0016/RS0017 fail the build until `PublicAPI.Unshipped.txt` names it, and the other streams read that file's diff. RS0026 (multiple overloads with optional parameters) flags every string/`ReadOnlySpan<char>` pair; the pairs are disambiguated by the first parameter's type and the optional parameters carry the Python keyword-argument ergonomics (`beginning`, `length`, `partial`, `timeout`) two S53b reviews confirmed. The rule Roslyn's guidance protects is adopted where it bites: **after 1.0, never add or reorder an optional parameter on a shipped overload; new capability is a new overload with all parameters required, or a new method.** Sources read 2026-09-16: Roslyn "Adding Optional Parameters in Public API"; Framework Design Guidelines, Member Overloading (2008 edition, marked possibly out of date).
- 2026-09-16 (owner): **the demo keeps the 2026-08-31 design, Vue 3 plus a Web Worker hosting the .NET WebAssembly runtime; Blazor reconsidered and declined again.** The owner's notes asked whether Blazor would ease including the WASM-compiled library; it does not, because the worker hosts the runtime directly through `[JSExport]`, and the worker is the safety design. Scope is staged: v1 the roadmap's small page, v2 every major feature with editable samples and contextual help, v3 polish. Assessment of all the owner's Phase 7-9 notes: `docs/plan/2026-09-16-owner-notes-phases-7-9.md`.
- 2026-09-16 (owner, Phase 7 ground rules): **measure first; no design decision on the span threading or the lazy-walk state shape until the benchmarks exist.** (a) Span threading through `MatchState` is not deferred long: because it touches most engine files, S58's measurement includes the span-copy cost so the decision is taken early on numbers, not on a guess. (b) The lazy-walk allocation shape (pooled state on `Dispose` versus a `ref struct` enumerator) is decided from the same measurements; no guessing beforehand. (c) **Structural divergence from upstream's shape is allowed**, on the condition that the notes for future `sync-upstream` work are strictly maintained and enforced by checklist and script so they cannot be forgotten (each such change records where the port and upstream no longer line up and how to re-align); the minimum measured gain that justifies one is deferred until concrete examples exist. (d) **Native AOT compatibility outranks AOT-specific optimisation**: benchmark on the JIT, benchmark under AOT only where the mechanism is JIT-dependent, keep the AOT publish green on every slice; optimisations that help both are the ideal.
- 2026-09-16 (owner): **the GBP 160 expiring 2026-09-19 is extra-usage credit on the Claude subscription**, drawn after the weekly cap; slice sessions bill that subscription. There is no CLI query for it from the orchestrator; the owner pastes `/usage` at the start of an evening's run and the driver's per-session dollar figures (`done:` lines) are summed against it.
- 2026-09-16 (S53b, process): **when a sitting's records disagree, the commit message wins, because it is written last - and the fix is to update the notes file in the same edit that writes the message.** S53b sitting 1 left `notes/S53b-sittings.md` saying the independent verifier never reported and its commit message saying the verifier confirmed ten of eleven claims, the full three-seed oracle alone outstanding. Sitting 2 had to read both to learn which was true, then re-ran only that one claim (CONFIRMED, 0 diverge at each of seeds 7, 4242, 20260916). A checkpoint's "what is left" belongs in one place, and STATE.md must agree with it.
- 2026-09-16 (S54, tooling): **BenchmarkDotNet cannot find its own project in this repository, and the fix is `bench/FuzzyRegex.Benchmarks.slnx` plus a working directory, not `--inProcess`.** BDN regenerates a project per job and locates the original by walking up from the WORKING DIRECTORY to the nearest `.sln`/`.slnx` and then searching every subdirectory below it for `FuzzyRegex.Benchmarks.csproj` (`CsProjGenerator.GetProjectFilePath`, source read 2026-09-16). Every git worktree under `.claude/worktrees/` is a checkout of this repository and carries a copy, so with the repository-root solution as the stop point the run dies with "Found more than one matching project file ... Benchmark project names needs to be unique" having executed zero benchmarks - measured from the repository root and from `bench/FuzzyRegex.Benchmarks`, with three worktrees checked out. A one-project solution at `bench/` stops the walk one level above the project, where exactly one copy lives: 32 of 32 benchmarks then ran on the DEFAULT out-of-process toolchain, so no accuracy is traded. It is the working directory that decides, which is why `tools/compare-benchmarks.ps1` owns the run and sets it. (S54 sitting 1 had reached for `--inProcess`, which works but measures in the host process and rules out the `--runtimes net10.0 net11.0` comparison the slice reserves for when a .NET 11 runtime is installed.)
- 2026-09-16 (S54): **S51's `MatchingBenchmarks` is deleted into the S54 suite.** It was three shapes written for one before/after on the cancellation check, which is done. `ReferenceBenchmarks.BacktrackingPort` keeps S51's exact pattern and subject - `(a|a)*b` over 18 characters - so that commit message stays comparable; its literal and fuzzy scans are superseded by the megabyte workloads. Carrying a near-duplicate class would have cost every Phase 7 comparison run three extra benchmarks for no new information.
- 2026-09-16 (S54, measured): **`(a+)+b` is not the catastrophic shape in this port; `(a|a)*b` is.** Timed with the benchmark project's `sizing` mode: `(a+)+b` answers in 0.67/0.21/0.36 ms at n=18/22/24, flat, because the inner `a+` is one greedy repeat with nothing to redistribute; `(a|a)*b` takes 160.92/2,649.52/10,646.58 ms at the same three lengths, doubling per character. `(a+)+b` does become slow eventually - 92 ms at n=200 and 86.5 s at n=2000 - but it is not exponential. Upstream answers every one of them instantly, because `locate_required_string` rejects a subject with no `b` before the engine runs (S19); that is the Phase 7 optimisation. Both shapes are pinned in `Gaps/Engine/OptimiserTrapsTests.cs`, and the timeout pin uses `(a|a)*b` at n=22 so that a timeout which stops firing costs 2.6 seconds rather than 86.
- 2026-09-16 (S54, measured): **the lazy walk's per-step state costs 113x at a megabyte and is quadratic in the subject.** Same sizing run: a full `EnumerateMatches` walk of `\w+` takes 12,643 ms over 1 MB against 111 ms for the eager `Matches`, and 117 ms over 100 KB against 4.51 ms - a 10.2x subject for 108x the lazy cost. `OPTIMISATION-NOTES.md` had the mechanism and no number; it now has both. The consequence for this slice is that nothing walks the megabyte lazily to the end: the benchmark and the trap test drain the lazy walk at 100 KB, and the megabyte lazy case is measured only where it stops after two matches (0.14 ms against 111 ms eager, which is what laziness buys).
- 2026-09-16 (S54): **the v1.0 performance gate's Python side is deliberately not baselined here.** The gate pairs this port's medians with Python `regex` medians taken on the same machine in the same session (`benchmark` skill), so a Python number recorded now could not be paired with any .NET number taken after Phase 7 optimises - it would have to be retaken anyway. S54 commits the .NET baselines and the `System.Text.RegularExpressions` reference ratios, which is what Phase 7 regresses against; the gate pairing belongs to the slice that closes the optimisation work.
- 2026-09-16 (S54, analyzers): **MA0009, S6444 and MA0023 are disapplied for `bench/**/*.cs` only; S125 was NOT disapplied.** MA0009 and S6444 want a `matchTimeout` on every `System.Text.RegularExpressions.Regex` built in the reference benchmarks, and MA0023 wants named groups or `ExplicitCapture` instead of `(\w+) (\w+)`. Every pattern there is a literal a few lines above its use, one of them is deliberately the catastrophic case because that IS the workload, a timeout would put a clock check inside the loop being measured, and the numbered groups are what the template benchmark exercises. Passing `Regex.InfiniteMatchTimeout` would have satisfied both rules while changing no risk at all, which is silencing rather than deciding. S125 ("remove this commented out code") fired eight times on the provenance comments in `OptimiserTrapsTests.cs`, which quote upstream calls the slice rule requires: every flagged line ended in a semicolon and so parsed as a statement, so the comments were reworded to end in commas and the rule stays on for the whole test suite.
- 2026-09-17 (S55, sitting 2): **Stryker.NET runs against this repo's TUnit/MTP suite; `-t mtp --target-framework net10.0` is the invocation.** Without `--target-framework`, Buildalyzer picks VS Build Tools' full-framework MSBuild and fails with MSB4276 (no .NET 10 SDK resolver there); the flag sidesteps the failing probe, no environment-variable workaround needed. An orchestrator message mid-sitting claimed Stryker refuses MTP projects outright and asked for the slice to be marked blocked with the tooling deleted; reproduced twice (this route and the orchestrator's own suggested `--msbuild-path` route) with real coverage capture and real killed mutants in both logs, no refusal message in either - the claim was false and was withdrawn. Evidence: `docs/plan/slices/notes/S55-sittings.md`. Also found: `-Mutate` composes only one glob at a time; more than one (repeated `-m`, or several array entries) silently mutates nothing, so `tools/run-stryker.ps1` and `tools/stryker-queue.json` both take exactly one glob per invocation.
- 2026-09-18 (owner): **Phase 9 (browser demo) moves ahead of 1.0, and Phases 8 and 9 start now in parallel worktrees (`docs`, `demo`) alongside Phase 6's mutation queue** - the owner wants the demo site to experiment with the library before launching it publicly. Spec amendment 26; Phase 8 sliced as S64-S69 the same day.
- 2026-09-18 (S64): **NuGet.org does not rewrite relative markdown links in a packaged README to the source repository - that is a third-party feature (`devlooped/readme`), not a built-in NuGet SDK behaviour.** Verified 2026-09-18 (Microsoft Learn's package-readme-on-nuget-org page states relative *image* paths simply do not render; a websearch on the link case surfaced only the third-party package as the mechanism). README.md's repo-relative links are therefore absolute `https://github.com/zejji/fuzzy-regex-cs/blob/main/...` URLs instead, so they resolve on both nuget.org and GitHub.
- 2026-09-18 (owner): **compile-time memory is bounded with a node budget (S56b, Opus), not by changing the unrolling; counted repeats without unrolling are a post-1.0 candidate.** The unrolling is inherited (upstream changelog 2018.11.22) and keeps the position-keyed repeat guard sound; measured growth is linear in the product of nested counts, identical to upstream within 5%. Investigation: docs/plan/2026-09-18-repeat-unrolling-investigation.md.
- 2026-09-18 (S70, measured): **the demo's engine-side safety is three caps and a stopwatch, and none of them covers pattern COMPILATION.** `EnumerateMatches`'s `timeout` is a per-STEP budget by design (`src/FuzzyRegex/FuzzyRegex.cs:947-951`), so a walk of a thousand matches was getting a thousand budgets - measured at 32 s returning success - and `DemoEngine.Search` now polls a `Stopwatch` deadline itself. A `CancellationTokenSource` would read better and would not work: in the browser the walk owns the worker's only thread until it returns, so the timer callback cannot run until the deadline no longer matters. `MaxMatches` alone left the ANSWER unbounded, because a capture list holds one span per repetition and nesting multiplies it (128 MB of JSON from a legal subject), so `MaxSpans = 50_000` budgets every group and capture in the whole answer. `flags` was the one stranger-controlled input with no cap at all, and the unknown-flag error quoted the offending token back in full, turning a 2 MB flag list into a 12 MB answer; hence `MaxFlagsLength = 200` and `MaxQuotedTokenLength = 40`. What remains uncovered is construction: `(((a{100}){100}){100}){100}` is inside `MaxPatternLength` and never returns, which is sliced separately as S56b and is the reason `worker.terminate()` - not the timeout - is the demo's real safety net, and therefore S71's primary control rather than an error path.
- 2026-09-18 (S70): **a browser harness must fail loudly, and a static `import` prevents that.** `worker.js` imports `_framework/dotnet.js` dynamically inside its try block: a static top-level import is evaluated before the module registers its `message` listener, so a missing runtime file aborted the worker before it could report anything and every request vanished silently - the page sat on "running..." forever, which reads as loading rather than broken. That confusion is the single thing this harness exists to prevent (a failed boot must be distinguishable from a failed render when S71's Vue page misbehaves), so the worker now answers a failed boot, guarded against firing twice or demoting a healthy worker, and `ask()` carries its own timeout. Related: a check that cannot fail is worse than no check - the `terminate()` proof passed with the `terminate()` call deleted, because it read its flag 500 ms in when the engine's own 2 s budget meant no answer could have arrived anyway; it now waits past that budget.
- 2026-09-18 (S65): **a naive `line.Split('|')` over a markdown table row is unsafe wherever a cell quotes a fuzzy pattern, because those patterns use `|` for alternation.** DIVERGENCES.md's "Inherited upstream bugs are fixed here" row quotes `` `I\|F` ``; splitting on every `|` desynchronised every cell after it and silently dropped the row from `ComparisonCoversDivergencesTests`'s scan (found by a negative control that unexpectedly passed). Fixed with a backtick-aware splitter (`SplitRowRespectingCodeSpans`) that only splits outside a code span. Any future markdown-table scan in this repo should use the same technique rather than a plain `Split('|')`.
- 2026-09-18 (S65): **checking a heading's text against a whole markdown file's raw text is vacuous wherever the file also cross-references that heading from a body cell.** COMPARISON.md's cross-reference tables quote a section's heading inside an ordinary body cell (`See "**There is no \`findall\`.**" below.`), so a check against the raw file text still passes after the section itself is deleted, as long as the cross-reference survives. Fixed by matching only against the file's own `##`/`###` heading lines (`ComparisonHeadingLines`), proven by a negative control that removed the "There is no `findall`" heading while leaving its cross-reference intact.
- 2026-09-18 (S66): **versioning is a hand-bumped `VersionPrefix`, not MinVer or a git-tag scheme.** There is exactly one release in flight at a time and no version tags exist yet, so MinVer's benefit - a distinct version per commit between tags - has nothing to attach to. `Directory.Build.props` sets `1.0.0`; `dotnet pack -p:VersionSuffix=rc.N` covers a rehearsal build.
- 2026-09-18 (S66): **`Authors` and `PackageProjectUrl` on `src/FuzzyRegex/FuzzyRegex.csproj`, flagged missing by S64, are filled in** (`Gerard Howell` from `NOTICE`'s copyright line; the GitHub repo URL, matching the existing `RepositoryUrl`). `PackageLicenseFile=LICENSE` stays as S64 decided it, not a gap. `Microsoft.SourceLink.GitHub` is added so a consumer's debugger can step into source, at the cost of three finding categories (project URL, repository URL, "source file not accessible") that only resolve once the packed commit is pushed to GitHub - `tools/pack-and-validate.ps1` legitimately fails on those three against an unpushed commit, by design.
- 2026-09-18 (S66): **`dotnet nuget verify` is not run, because it requires a signed package and this project has no signing certificate.** Verified 2026-09-18: `dotnet nuget verify` on a freshly packed `.nupkg` fails `NU3004` ("The package is not signed"). Revisit if the owner sets up code signing before 1.0.
- 2026-09-18 (S66): **no package icon exists; `IconMustBeSet` is excluded from `meziantou.validate-nuget-package` rather than faked with placeholder art.** A branding decision for the owner, not a packaging gap - add `<PackageIcon>` and drop the exclusion once artwork exists.
- 2026-09-18 (S66, caught mid-slice): **`dotnet add package`/`dotnet new` run inside any scratch folder under this repo's tree can silently write into the REAL root `Directory.Build.props`/`Directory.Packages.props`.** MSBuild's central-package-management search climbs upward from wherever the new project sits and stops at the first props file found; a scratch consumer project with no local override climbs straight to the repo root. `tools/run-release-rehearsal.ps1`'s first run landed a stray `<PackageVersion Include="FuzzyRegex" Version="1.0.0-rc.0" />` in the real `Directory.Packages.props` this way. Fixed by writing empty `<Project />` overrides into the scratch folder before `dotnet new`/`dotnet add package` run. Any future tooling that creates a throwaway project under this repo needs the same guard.
- 2026-09-18 (S55, closing): **S55 closed with zero survivors across all three in-scope Stryker chunks** (API layer 237/237, parser 2156 Killed+29 Timeout+4 RuntimeError of 2189, `Engine/Substitution.cs` 261/261). The 4 RuntimeError mutants are stack-overflow-causing recursion-bound removals in `ParseFunctions.cs` (`ParseSetItem`'s version-1 nested-set guard, `FloatToRational`'s continued-fraction convergence invariant) - correctly detected, not survivors. Full numbers: `docs/plan/mutation/2026-09-17-api-parser.md`. The independent verifier pass caught 6 real inaccuracies a prior blind review missed (a ratchet-not-rerun claim that was in fact rerun, a stale engine-queue file list and chunk count, an unlogged StackOverflowException stated as fact, unsupported hand-check provenance, an overclaimed Ignored-reason label) - all fixed before commit, evidence that the two-pass process catches different classes of error.
- 2026-09-18 (S67): **The GitHub repo `zejji/fuzzy-regex-cs` is currently private or not yet pushed** - `curl https://api.github.com/repos/zejji/fuzzy-regex-cs` returns 404 unauthenticated (GitHub gives the same 404 for both cases). Context7's public submission and DeepWiki's no-account flow both need it public; `docs/plan/FINDABILITY.md` carries this as a prerequisite rather than a submission step, since neither can be attempted until it is resolved.
- 2026-09-18 (S56b): **The compile path is bounded by a node budget, `maxCompiledNodes`, defaulting to 1,000,000 nodes (about 250 MB, about 0.7 s), checked at `NodeCompiler.CreateNode` and refused with `FuzzyRegexParseException`.** The budget counts nodes CREATED, not nodes kept: `Optimiser.DiscardUnusedNodes` prunes about half a counted repeat's graph, but only after the memory has been spent, so `(a{100}){100}` creates 20,913 nodes while keeping 10,509 and a budget of 10,509 does not compile it. There is deliberately no "unlimited" setting, and upstream's unbounded behaviour is not reproducible at any setting (`int.MaxValue` nodes is about 500 GB). Rationale and measurements: `docs/DIVERGENCES.md` "Compile budget" row.
- 2026-09-18 (S56b): **The slice's test (2) is not implementable as written and was changed, not the default.** It expected `(a{1000}){1000}` to "still compile at the default budget"; that pattern KEEPS 1,005,009 nodes and creates about twice that, so at a default of 1,000,000 it is refused. Raising the default to admit it would mean agreeing to half a gigabyte of graph for one pattern, which is the shape the slice exists to refuse. `CompileBudgetTests` uses `(a{100000})?b` and `(a{1000}){100}` instead, both with pinned upstream answers.
- 2026-09-18 (S56b): **The internal "compile under this version" seam is a named factory, `internal static FuzzyRegex.WithDefaultVersion(...)`, not an internal constructor.** Adding the optional trailing `int maxCompiledNodes` to the public constructor silently rebound both 5-argument call sites (`tests/FuzzyRegex.Tests/Ported/Upstream.cs`, `tests/FuzzyRegex.OracleTests/OracleComparer.cs`) from the internal constructor's `defaultVersion` to the new budget - C# prefers a candidate that omits no optional parameters - so `RegexFlags.Version0` became a budget of 8,192 nodes. It compiled, and surfaced as two ratchet failures and three oracle divergences that looked like engine regressions. A name cannot be captured by an overload; any future optional parameter on this family needs every positional call site checked.
- 2026-09-18 (S70, sitting 3): **a check script that can be pointed at output it did not produce says so in its verdict, not only in its parameters.** `tools/run-wasm-smoke.ps1 -SkipPublish` exists because the missing-artefact branch was unreachable while the script published before it checked - the publish restored whatever you deleted to test it. A `-SkipPublish` run's banner names what it did not check, because a verdict line byte-identical to a real run's is a log nobody can read backwards.
- 2026-09-18 (S70, sitting 3): **a probe asserting that a failure branch fired must prove it could have said otherwise.** The first version of `tools/probes/wasm-smoke-missing-artefact.ps1` tested "non-zero exit and the filename appears in the output", which the integrity-MISMATCH branch satisfies just as well, and it never checked the publish was green before perturbing it. `tools/probes/wasm-smoke-branch-controls.ps1` stages both mistakes deliberately and is committed beside it: describing a control in prose is what left S18's and S22's unreproducible.
- 2026-09-18 (S70, sitting 3): **kill order matters when a stray server holds a publish directory open.** PID 37516's `python -m http.server` blocks the clean publish, because the directory cannot be deleted - but publishing INTO a locked directory works, and that same server is the one already serving the harness. So the browser leg runs first and the kill comes after. Two sittings have now ended with the browser half unrun: a slice whose done-criteria need a session tool should test for that tool before doing the work that depends on it.
- 2026-09-18 (S70, sitting 4): **when another process holds the publish directory, publish elsewhere rather than kill it.** `tools/run-wasm-smoke.ps1 -OutDir <dir>` clears `obj/` and that directory and leaves `bin/` alone, which is enough for the zero-trim-warning claim: `obj/` is where the linker's state lives, `bin/` only ever mattered because the publish landed there. This is the general answer to the kill-order problem the entry above records - the server serving the browser leg never has to die for a clean publish to happen. `-OutDir` refuses a directory containing the repository, because the clean step deletes what it is given.
- 2026-09-18 (S70, sitting 4): **a zero-trim-warning claim states how it knows the trimmer ran.** A publish that skips the linker also emits no trim warnings, and the log at normal verbosity never names ILLink. The evidence recorded is `obj/Release/net10.0/linked/Link.semaphore`'s timestamp falling inside the run whose first act was deleting `obj/`.
- 2026-09-18 (S70, sitting 4): **a worktree session that runs the main checkout's copy of a script gets a plausible-looking wrong answer, not an error.** `pwsh -File C:\...\fuzzy-regex-cs\tools\check-ratchet.ps1` from the demo worktree built and ran MAIN's test assembly (6343 passed) and then failed only on `Ratchet: RED - no test report was produced`, because it looked for the report under the main checkout. Run tooling by a path inside the worktree.
- 2026-09-18 (S70, sitting 4): **an artefact check asks both directions, and a compressed variant is an artefact.** `tools/run-wasm-smoke.ps1` now checks that every file the manifest names is on disk (`.br` and `.gz` included - the skip that excluded them from hashing was excluding them from existence too, and 41 of 42 could be deleted with the script still GREEN) and that nothing is on disk the manifest does not name (an orphan from an earlier build inflated the printed size baseline from 22 files/6.94 MB to 23/8.36 MB, silently). `tools/probes/wasm-smoke-artefact-checks.ps1` holds both down with a control either side.
- 2026-09-18 (S70, sitting 4): **the bounded-probe heap cap goes on demo-engine probes, not on the test suite.** S70's independent verifier was briefed to set `DOTNET_GCHeapHardLimit=0x40000000` on every dotnet process, ran `tools/check-ratchet.ps1` under it, and got RED at 6341 passing / 2 failing: `FuzzyRecursionTests.The_stack_bound_is_still_what_catches_a_blowup_the_guard_cannot_see` and `InheritedIssueTests.A_long_repeated_capture_group_costs_the_backtracking_stack_a_block_per_repetition`, both `OutOfMemoryException` in `ByteStack.Grow` (`ByteStack.cs:306`). Those two tests exist to grow the backtracking stack until something stops them, so under a 1 GB cap the cap is what stops them. Uncapped, the ratchet is GREEN at 6343/0/0. A RED ratchet under the cap is a measurement artefact, not a regression.
- 2026-09-18 (S71, sitting 1): **the demo ships no `<base href>` at all**, against both the slice file's own plan and Microsoft's Blazor-on-Pages recipe (which rewrites it with a third-party Action). Every URL in the page is document-relative and the worker is resolved against `import.meta.url`, so one artefact boots at the repository subpath on Pages, at the root of a local server and at a fork's preview path, with nothing to keep in step. A hard-coded base is precisely the "works at the subpath, breaks at the root" failure the slice file set out to hunt. `checks.html` is run both ways in the browser leg to hold it down.
- 2026-09-18 (S71, sitting 1): **the eight sidebar examples' expected answers come from upstream `regex 2026.9.10` under VERSION1**, recorded in `tools/probes/demo-examples-expectations.py` and pinned by `DemoExamplesTests`. Upstream's module default is VERSION0 and this port's is VERSION1 (a documented divergence), so a probe run at the module default would pin answers the demo does not give. Every expectation in that file carries the call that produced it.
- 2026-09-18 (S71, sitting 1): **the demo's Node tests run in `pages.yml`, not `ci.yml`.** The demo gates nothing: a broken page must never block a library merge, and the tests are only meaningful to the artefact the same workflow then publishes. The demo's C# tests are ordinary tests in the ordinary suite and need no special wiring.
- 2026-09-18 (S71, sitting 1): **`pages.yml` triggers on `src/**` as well as `demo/**`**, widening the slice's stated trigger list. The demo compiles the library into its artefact, so a library change with no demo change still changes what is deployed; watching only `demo/**` leaves the live page running an older engine than `main` with nothing to say so.
- 2026-09-18 (S71, sitting 2): **the no-base-href decision is now tested, not argued.** `checks.html` is CHECKS GREEN (9 of 9) at `http://localhost:8090/checks.html` and again at `http://localhost:8091/fuzzy-regex-cs/checks.html` - the same artefact boots and answers identically at a server root and at the repository subpath GitHub Pages will serve. Sitting 1's divergence from Microsoft's recipe stands on evidence.
- 2026-09-18 (S71, sitting 2): **a browser check may not assert on `requestAnimationFrame` on a page that runs WebAssembly workers.** Measured in Chrome 153 under the Playwright MCP server, two 500 ms windows on each of two *idle* pages with `visibilityState === 'visible'`: a page with nothing on it gave 31 and 31 frames, while the demo page - which boots two .NET WebAssembly workers - gave 1 and then 0, its timers ticking 28 and 30 throughout. The browser does drive rAF; it does not drive it for that page, cause unestablished. Liveness is therefore asserted with 16 ms timer ticks on both the checks page's thread and the page-under-test's (32 and 32 in a 500 ms window while a runaway ran), plus the page's status pills read mid-runaway. The frame count is still reported, never asserted on.
- 2026-09-18 (S71, sitting 2): **assigning an iframe `src` that differs only in its fragment is a same-document navigation** - no reload, no `load` event, and a driver awaiting one waits for ever. `checks.html` blanks the frame to `about:blank` and awaits that load first. This is why the checks page hung at check 2 the first time it was ever run in a browser.
- 2026-09-18 (S71, sitting 2): **a fragment written with `URLSearchParams` must be read back with `URLSearchParams`.** It encodes a space as `+`, which `decodeURIComponent` does not decode, so the address-bar check failed against a page that was behaving correctly. Parsing it back is also what the page itself does on load.
- 2026-09-18 (S71, sitting 2): **the Vite/TypeScript/Tailwind re-plan (amendment 27) was not started, because `npm` is not in the driver session's Bash allowlist** and writing an uninstalled, un-type-checked, untested project would be unverified code. The sandbox reaches `registry.npmjs.org` (HTTP 200), Node v24.16.0 is installed, and today's registry versions are recorded in the sitting-2 notes for pinning. Self-granting the permission was not done: widening the sandbox is the owner's call.
- 2026-09-18 (S71): the demo front end is a Vite project at `demo/web` that builds INTO the .NET web root (`emptyOutDir: false`, an explicit two-entry clean), so one publish and one static-asset manifest still cover page, worker and runtime.
- 2026-09-18 (S71): `typescript` pinned to 6.0.3, not 7.0.2 - `vue-tsc@3.3.11` declares `typescript >=5.0.0` but crashes under 7 with `ERR_PACKAGE_PATH_NOT_EXPORTED: Package subpath './lib/tsc'`. A declared peer range is not evidence about a layout change.
- 2026-09-18 (S71): the worker is resolved against `document.baseURI`, not `import.meta.url`, because a bundled module sits under `assets/`; this keeps the no-base-href property that makes one artefact boot at a root and at `/fuzzy-regex-cs/`.
- 2026-09-18 (S71): `checks.html` takes `createPool` and `spawnEngineWorker` from `window.__demoInternals` instead of importing modules - a hashed bundle has no stable module URL - which also measures respawn in the window the demo's workers are spawned from.
- 2026-09-18 (S71): the smoke script no longer names the page's assets; it parses the published `index.html` and asserts every `assets/...` it references exists, which fails on a stale page as well as a missing one.
- 2026-09-19 (S71): the dev middleware that serves the .NET publish is a PRE hook, not a post hook, because Vite's `htmlFallbackMiddleware` rewrites the URL to `/index.html` for any `Accept: text/html` or wildcard request - which is what `new Worker()` sends - so a post hook never saw `/worker.js` and the demo could not boot under `npm run dev`.
- 2026-09-19 (S71): the same middleware declines this project's own build output (`index.html`, `assets/`), case-insensitively and re-tested against `realpathSync.native`, because its last fallback root IS the build output directory; without that the dev server serves the last production bundle, and NTFS reaches it again through an 8.3 short name.
- 2026-09-19 (S71): a test that guards a gitignored artefact creates the artefact itself. `demo/web/tests/dev-server.test.ts` writes its own shadow `index.html` before booting the server, because on a fresh checkout - the Pages workflow's state - there is no build output to shadow the source page and the test could never go red.
- 2026-09-19 (S71): `*.js binary` deliberately does not extend to `.mjs`. It protects `wwwroot/worker.js`, the one committed file a browser fetches under an integrity attribute; `demo/web/scripts/clean.mjs` runs on the build machine, so it stays text and keeps readable diffs.
- 2026-09-19 (S71): `docs/STATUS.md`'s upstream commit is only trustworthy where the `upstream` submodule is checked out - `git -C upstream rev-parse HEAD` walks up to the parent repository otherwise and returns our own HEAD, which is how `611be7e3` (our merge commit) was committed as the upstream commit. Fixed here by initialising the submodule; the generator still needs to fail loudly.
- 2026-09-19 (S72): partial matching is a demo MODE, not a flag on the walk, because upstream's scanning functions take no `partial` argument and neither do this port's - a partial match is only ever the last thing a search finds, so "every match, and the last one may be partial" is not a question the engine answers.
- 2026-09-19 (S72): `DemoEngine.Run` gained three arguments rather than an options object, because everything crossing `[JSExport]` is a string and six strings marshal as one shape. AMENDED by the S72 review the same day: the three-argument overload that was kept beside it "so a browser holding the cached S71 page keeps getting the S71 answer" could never do that - the `[JSExport]` the browser calls went from three parameters to six in the same change, so a cached page fails on arity before reaching any overload. It had no production caller and is deleted; the tests that wanted the short form declare it as their own helper.
- 2026-09-19 (S72): the demo's help panels are GENERATED from `docs/COMPARISON.md` at build time and gitignored, and `tools/build-demo-help.ps1` fails when a heading it quotes is renamed. A committed copy would be a second source of the same prose, and the failure is what keeps documentation and demo the same words.
- 2026-09-19 (S72): the generated help is blocks of plain/`code` runs, not markdown, so the page renders it with Vue text interpolation - no markdown renderer, and nothing in COMPARISON.md can inject markup into the page.
- 2026-09-19 (S72): `tools/check-doc-examples.ps1` had been red on main since dfa8767 (S56b, 2026-09-18): the `maxCompiledNodes` example used an undeclared `untrustedPattern` and expected an output ending in `...`, which the checker compares literally. Fixed in this commit because the slice's own new sections could not be verified until it was green.
- 2026-09-19 (S72): `dotnet csharpier check .` had been red on main since e38f270 (S71) on `FuzzyRegex.Tests.csproj` and `FuzzyRegex.Demo.Wasm.csproj` - whitespace only. Reformatted here rather than in a separate commit because a formatting gate that is already red cannot tell this slice's own files apart.
- 2026-09-19 (S72): the highlight paints matches in SUBJECT order while each keeps the number the answer gave it, and the page finds a mark or a row by `data-match` rather than by position, because a RightToLeft search numbers the LAST match in the subject first - painting order and match numbering are two different orders and only one of them is the DOM's.
- 2026-09-19 (S72): two matches at the same index are painted SHORTEST first. A reverse search answers with an empty match at a longer match's start index (`a*` over "baa" upstream: (1,3), (1,1), (0,0)), and an empty match fits before the longer one begins; the overlap guard is for matches that share a character, which no engine walk produces.
- 2026-09-19 (S72): the demo's timeout sample is `^(a|aa)+$` over 'a'*36 + 'b', not `(a+a+)+b` over 'a'*30, because the latter answers in 1 ms under `regex 2026.9.10` and 75 ms here - a sample that advertises the timeout has to be exponential in both engines, or it advertises a weakness of this port instead.
- 2026-09-19 (S72): every array `return` in `tools/build-demo-help.ps1` carries the leading comma (`return , @($x)`). A one-element array unrolls through PowerShell's pipeline into its element, which produced a `help.json` whose single-run paragraphs were objects and which the page's validator rejected whole.
- 2026-09-19 (S72): `demo/wwwroot/checks.html` keeps no copy of the upstream spans. It asserts that every example in the sidebar reaches the engine and is answered - and that the slow one is refused - and names `tests/FuzzyRegex.Tests/Gaps/Demo/DemoExamplesTests.cs` as where the numbers live, so the browser verdict page cannot become a second, hand-maintained source of upstream answers. (Written as an intention first: the page shipped with all seventeen span sets copied into it, and the S72 review found the decision and the file disagreeing. The spans are out.)
- 2026-09-19 (S72): contrast ratios for the demo are measured by resolving the computed colour through a 1x1 canvas, not by parsing it - `oklch(...)` is not sRGB text and parsing it as such returns a nonsense 1.01:1.
- 2026-09-19 (S73): the demo's copy rules are DATA (`demo/web/tests/copy-rules.ts`), each rule carrying the source that asked for it, and every rule fires on a form rather than a judgement. "Restating the obvious" is in the slice's ban list and is deliberately not a rule: no pattern separates a sentence that repeats its label from one that adds what the label leaves out, and a rule that guesses is a rule the next person switches off.
- 2026-09-19 (S73): the linted sources are the strings a VISITOR reads - `examples.json`, `App.vue` (static and bound attributes), `demo.ts`, `src/lib`, `DemoEngine.cs`, `index.html`, the help generator's own note. Repository documents, `demo/README.md` included, are not linted: nobody reading the page reads them, and interface-copy rules would ban the words maintainer prose needs ("deploy", "key difference"). `DemoEngine.cs` is in because its refusals land in the same `role="status"` paragraph as the page's own messages.
- 2026-09-19 (S73): the demo's refusals name what to do, not what the input was not - `Unknown mode 'split'. The modes are ...` rather than `'split' is not a mode`. `DemoEngineContractTests` and `shapes.test.ts` pin the new wording.
- 2026-09-19 (S73): the demo's fixed shell is gated on `(min-width: 64rem) and (min-height: 600px)`, and the two panes' `overflow-y: auto` lives inside that same block. Width alone is not enough: at 1366x500 - a laptop at 200 % zoom, a phone in landscape - a width-only gate gave a scrolling document with two scrolling panes inside it, three stacked scroll regions. Below either line the page is an ordinary document, which is also what WCAG 1.4.10 Reflow asks for.
- 2026-09-19 (S73): `body` is painted in the INK (`bg-shell text-shell-text`) and the white results pane names its own `text-slate-900`, not the other way round. The shell is most of the page, so a word that names no colour of its own must be legible on the dark surface; the inverse shipped three unreadable mode labels. `contrast.test.ts` now requires the `bg-`/`text-` pair of each inheriting region to be one of the measured pairs.
- 2026-09-19 (S73): demo colour is a test and not a recorded figure - `tests/colour.ts` (CSS Color 4 sections 9 and 10, WCAG 2.2 luminance) plus 22 measured pairs in `contrast.test.ts`, and a per-token record of what Chrome actually paints, so the arithmetic is checked against a browser rather than against itself. That record caught three tokens already in the tree that were OUTSIDE the sRGB gamut (`accent-soft`, `hit-a`, `hit-edge`): a browser gamut-maps rather than clips, so S72's recorded hit ratios described a colour nobody sees.
- 2026-09-19 (S73): `checks.html` asserts on the NUMBERS a message carries, not on its phrasing. Its cap check matched the literal `"limit of"` and went red when chunk 1 reworded the refusal to "the demo's limit is 100,000" - a red verdict page with nothing wrong behind it. A check coupled to copy fails every time the copy improves, and the cap is the part a visitor actually needs.
- 2026-09-19 (S73): a collapsed region in the demo is `hidden`, never dropped from the DOM, and both tab panels are always rendered. APG's Tabs pattern requires each `tab` to have `aria-controls` referring to its `tabpanel`, and the one-panel-at-a-time version left the unselected tab pointing at an id that was not there; a disclosure whose target had gone was the same fault. `hidden` takes the region out of the accessibility tree as well as off the screen, and Tailwind's reset gives it `!important`, so a display utility on the same element cannot reopen it.
- 2026-09-19 (S73): `styles.css` carries `@source not '../tests'`. Tailwind scans the test files too, so a sentence in `layout.test.ts` explaining why the page no longer uses `lg:overflow-y-auto` put that utility in the shipped stylesheet (25.32 kB to 24.14 kB once excluded). It also lets a test ask the BUILT stylesheet what the page contains without reading its own prose back - which is how the `min-height: 100vh` under the gate's `height: 100dvh` was found, after a source-string test had missed it.
- 2026-09-19 (S58): a noise run is only valid if NOTHING else on the machine builds or tests - another git worktree is not another CPU - and if the measuring session itself stays silent while it runs. Run B was ruined by a review pass in the demo worktree (08:47-09:50, orchestrator-confirmed) and run C by this session's own note-writing, which costs 2.6 cores through the Headroom context proxy per request. The five rules are in `bench/baselines/<machine-id>/noise-floor.md`.
- 2026-09-19 (S58): a load average does not qualify a benchmark run. `.scratch/load-guard.log` read `avg=1% peak=7%` throughout a window where 19 of 49 benchmarks were 1.4-2.4x slow, because a five-minute mean over 20 cores cannot see one core taken for ninety seconds. `tools/probes/sample-machine-load.ps1` samples per-process CPU deltas every 15 seconds instead, and its log is committed beside the run it qualifies.
- 2026-09-19 (S58): a discarded benchmark run is kept, not deleted (`2026-09-19-S58-noise-B-discarded.json`), because the evidence that the contention signal caught a bad run is worth more than the tidiness. `tools/probes/compare-two-baselines.ps1` reads any two committed baselines and prints each run's own min/median beside the ratio.
- 2026-09-19 (S58): the noise floor is 1.13 on time and 1.0001 on allocation, from runs A and E of an unchanged tree. The time floor comes from the IMPROVEMENT side - `ReverseFailedScan` ran 0.88916 of A with no code between the runs, and 1/0.88916 = 1.1247 is wider than the worst slowdown, 1.0768 - because an unexplained improvement inside the band is the same measurement artefact as a regression inside it. Allocation needs almost no floor: 38 of 45 allocating rows matched to the byte.
- 2026-09-19 (S58): a detached benchmark run is written into STATE.md with its PID, log path and expected finish BEFORE it is launched. Run D was ruined because the sitting that launched it was killed a minute later without recording it, so the next sitting read "take the one missing noise run" as an instruction to start one and spent three minutes orienting - which is itself 3.6-4.3 cores through the Headroom proxy, and its first eleven rows are 1.10-2.06x.
- 2026-09-19 (S58): three `check-sync-divergence` tests had never been run and were failing on the fixture, not the script: a here-string ends at the newline before `'@`, so every appended row was glued onto the markdown table's separator line and the script correctly saw a ledger with no rows. Two further tests were passing for that same wrong reason.
- 2026-09-19 (S58): an oracle divergence found on a date-seeded wave is NOT pinned as `expected` to get a green run. Pinning before deciding which engine is right cements whichever answer happened to be there; the row is written up with its reproduction instead (`2026-09-19-oracle-divergence-fuzzy-edit-attribution.md`).
- 2026-09-19 (S58): the noise floors move no verdict at these numbers, and that is left as it is. `compare-benchmarks.ps1` fails a run on `-Threshold` (1.25, both axes) and a floor can only EXCUSE a ratio that is over `-Threshold`; a floor below `-Threshold` therefore excuses nothing, so with floors of 1.13 and 1.0001 a row allocating 1.20x its baseline is GREEN - confirmed by scaling one row in a copy of run A's artifacts, and pinned as a test. Tightening the allocation threshold is a gate decision, so it goes to S63 as scope item 7 rather than being slipped into a measurement slice.
- 2026-09-19 (S58): allocation attribution for Phase 7 is arithmetic - `MemoryDiagnoser` bytes against the named allocation sites - because an unattended session can capture an allocation profile and cannot read one. Measured, not assumed: `dottrace` 2026.2.2 has no report verb and ships no `Reporter.exe`, the Rider MCP server was `ConnectionRefused`, and `dotnet-trace convert --format speedscope` over a `gc-verbose` trace yields an evented profile weighted in MILLISECONDS (3 threads, 144 events, heaviest leaf `CPU_TIME`) with no allocation weights, so PROFILING.md's route 2 is struck through. Reading `GCAllocationTick` payloads means writing a TraceEvent program.
- 2026-09-19 (S58): `dotnet-trace collect --profile gc-verbose -- <bench.exe> -i ...` deadlocks - the child sits at 6.08 s CPU and flat, the trace frozen at 161 KB, the BenchmarkDotNet log empty - while the same command without the tracer finishes normally. Attach to an already-running process with `--process-id` instead, allowing a few seconds' sleep, and give it a run long enough to attach to: a `--job short` single benchmark ends in six seconds and dotnet-trace then fails with `Process with an Id of <pid> is not running.`
- 2026-09-19 (S73): a layout test asserts over the stylesheet the browser RECEIVES, not the one the repository holds. `demo/web/tests/built-css.ts` runs Vite's JavaScript API in process (`configFile: false`, `write: false`, the same Tailwind plugin) and its output is byte-identical to `npm run build` - 24,144 characters, md5 `ebc4e84e067ef6dbfc926c70e5a4984a`, in about 130 ms, re-runnable from `tools/probes/demo-built-css-matches-production.test.ts`. The source-reading version both failed on edits that changed nothing (two tests broke on merging two identical `overflow-y` rules into one selector list) and passed while the shipped shell was sized in `100vh`, which is the bug chunk 2 shipped.
- 2026-09-19 (S73): a window that narrows past the shell's media gate opens whichever disclosure holds the focus, rather than re-focusing something afterwards. Vue's default pre-flush watcher runs before the DOM update, so `document.activeElement` is still the focused control and the region is never hidden at all - the focus does not move, which is what WCAG 3.2.2 asks, and there is no focus restoration to get wrong.
- 2026-09-19 (S73): which disclosure the gate opened is remembered per region (`openedByGate`), not as one flag, and a visitor's press clears ownership only of the region they pressed. Both halves are load-bearing and neither was tested until a mutation run said so: with the rules written for the samples panel alone, five mutants survived the suite - dropping the `=== which` guard, narrowing it to samples, reverting one `@click` to the inline toggle, and deleting the `advanced` line of the widen-close. `tests/layout.test.ts` therefore runs every ownership rule as `test.each` over both regions.
- 2026-09-19 (S73): the snippet panel's C# literals use three forms, not two. A value holding a
  carriage return, U+0085, U+2028 or U+2029 cannot be a raw string literal - C# ends a line on all
  four, so `a\r\nb` came back from the compiler as `a\nb` and a U+2028 in an indented one is
  `error CS8999` - so those values take an ordinary escaped literal instead, one line, every
  character spelt out. Reachable from the page through the fragment (`#s=a%0D%0Ab`), not through the
  textarea. Evidence: `tools/probes/demo-snippet-compiles.mjs`, case "a carriage return, U+2028,
  U+2029 and U+0085", prints `subject OK`.
- 2026-09-19 (S73): the snippet reads the mode the way `DemoEngine.TryParseMode` does
  (`Trim().ToLowerInvariant()`), because the fragment carries it as typed and `#m=Partial` is a case
  the engine answers - compared exactly, the panel showed a walk under a partial answer.
- 2026-09-19 (S73): the snippet generator trims the way .NET trims, not the way JavaScript does.
  `tools/probes/demo-trim-matches-dotnet.mjs` compiles and runs the C# that walks the BMP:
  `char.IsWhiteSpace` is true for 25 code points, .NET trims U+0085 and JavaScript does not,
  JavaScript trims U+FEFF and .NET does not, the other twenty-four agree. `snippet.ts` therefore has
  a `trimmed()` of its own at all four places `DemoEngine` trims, so `#m=partial%C2%85` shows a
  partial answer with a partial snippet rather than a walk. The same probe drives all 65,531
  reachable code units through `toCSharp` and reports 0 disagreements.
- 2026-09-19 (S73): `snippet.ts`'s `trimmed()` is an index walk and must not go back to
  `^[ws]+|[ws]+$`. The trailing alternative restarts inside every interior whitespace run, which is
  quadratic: over three runs of `tools/probes/demo-trim-matches-dotnet.mjs` on 2026-09-19 the regex
  took 101-422 ms for 12.5k characters of U+00A0 and 5.4-10.3 s for 100k, four times per doubling,
  against 0-2 ms for the walk at every size, and the panel regenerates on every keystroke. Pinned by
  `snippet.test.ts > a long run of whitespace does not freeze the panel` (200 ms budget).
- 2026-09-19 (S73): a non-ASCII whitespace character in `demo/web` source is written as an escape
  (`\u{a0}`, `\u{2028}`), never as the character itself. A raw U+00A0 renders as a space in every
  reader: it sent this sitting's blind reviewer and then its independent verifier to opposite wrong
  conclusions about the same test in one afternoon. The `u` flag is what makes `\u{...}` legal in a
  regex literal, so the flag stays.
- 2026-09-19 (S73): the snippet deliberately omits the page's display caps. `DemoEngine` passes
  `count: MaxMatches` to `Replace` and stops its walk at 1,000 matches; 1,000 is this page's limit
  and not the library's, and a visitor's own code should rewrite the whole subject. The page says
  out loud when it truncates. Pinned by `snippet.test.ts > replace mode prints the rewritten
  subject`, which asserts the whole snippet, so a `count:` argument would fail it.
- 2026-09-19 (S73): an arrow key in either half of the answer moves the selection and the focus and
  does not bring the other half into view; Enter, Space and a click do. Both halves share one scroll
  container, so a counterpart a screen away can only be shown by scrolling the focused control off
  screen. Measured in Chrome at 1366x768 with 80 matches: the reveal scrolled the pane to the
  counterpart and the focus call scrolled it straight back, so the reveal was a cancelled animation.
  Keeping the focus visible is WCAG 2.4.3. Pinned by `page.test.ts > the keyboard reaches both views
  of a match`; plant the reveal back in `rove()` to see the pin fail.
- 2026-09-19 (S73): `layout.test.ts > nothing but a link is underlined` reads the compiled
  stylesheet with regular expressions rather than parsing it, and rejects a Tailwind `underline`
  utility even on a real link, because `.underline` names no element. Links are underlined in
  `styles.css` against `a`. The ceiling and the lift (`postcss`, already in the tree) are a
  `SHORTCUT:` in the test.
